The Cat In The Hat Is Another Box Office Flop Warner Bros. Can't Afford

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Another weekend, another bad result for Warner Bros. "The Cat in the Hat" looked like it could help get Warner Bros. back on track at the box office. Instead? It became the latest in a string of flops that has defined the studio's year. Family-friendly movies have been very safe bets in recent years, and on paper, Dr. Seuss is very good intellectual property to exploit for a PG animated movie. But this didn't pan out as anyone had hoped.

Director Erica Rivinoja's "The Cat in the Hat" opened to a very disappointing $20 million domestically. It also pulled in just $10 million internationally, giving it a soft $30 million global start. That was only good enough for the number two spot, since "Godzilla Minus Zero" ruled the box office with a $30.5 million opening. Normally, it would be much safer to bet on the PG family movie over the R-rated Japanese monster movie. But this wasn't a normal situation, and instead, the animated movie was left fighting for scraps, relatively speaking.

The new movie sees the title character (voiced by "SNL" alum Bill Hader) take on his toughest assignment yet for the I.I.I.I. (Institute for the Institution of Imagination and Inspiration, LLC): to cheer up Gabby and Sebastian, a pair of siblings struggling with their move to a new town. It carries a reported budget in the $90 million range.

This major disappointment comes mere weeks after the Warner Bros. Discovery and Paramount merger was made official, with the mega-corporation Skydance now ruling over both studios. That corporation is lousy with debt and it needs Warner Bros. to deliver. Unfortunately for CEO David Ellison and his new film chiefs, they're walking into a messy situation. They've got a ship to right.

The storied movie studio had a stellar year in 2025, with massive hits like "Sinners" and "F1" to its name. Warner Bros. set a huge record that year with six movies in a row opening to at least $40 million, but 2026 has not been nearly as kind.

Skydance inherited a string of massive flops from the Warner Bros. deal, with its 2027 slate also looking a bit uneven. Things could get worse before they get better, as evidenced by the performance of "The Cat in the Hat." Granted, the year should end on a high note as "Dune: Part 3" is just around the corner, but even that can't make up for everything that preceded it.

A very rough rule of thumb for theatrical success has always been a movie making 2.5 times its budget, given that theaters keep around half of all money from ticket sales. So just how bad has the year been for Warner Bros? The studio's bombs/disappointments include "The Bride!" ($90 million budget / $24 million worldwide haul), "They Will Kill You" ($10 million budget / $19 million worldwide), "Mortal Kombat II" ($80 million budget / $129 million worldwide), "Supergirl" ($170 million budget / $126 million worldwide), "The End of Oak Street" ($90 million budget / $121 million worldwide), "Practical Magic 2" ($75 million budget / $106 million worldwide), and "Digger" ($180 million budget / $50 million worldwide).

Yikes. Tom Cruise's "Digger" alone will lose as much as $250 million. Hits like "Wuthering Heights" and "Clayface," encouraging though they may be, can't come close to making up for losses that pile up for would-be blockbusters like "Supergirl." Unfortunately, this Dr. Seuss adaptation now joins this ever-growing list of crushing disappointments. Skydance and the new team has their work cut out for them.

How Nick Cannon Really Feels About Mariah Carey Prenup

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ten years after Nick Cannon and Mariah Carey finalized their divorce, the  America’s Got Talent alum, who was married to the singer from 2008 to 2016, revealed the details of their prenup.

Boy, if you wanna know whether Nick Cannon and Mariah Carey had a prenup—he’ll tell you.

Ten years after the former couple—who share 15-year-old twins Moroccan and Monroe—finalized their divorce, the former America’s Got Talent host confirmed they “absolutely” signed a prenuptial agreement before tying the knot in 2008.

“Mariah Carey, her entity, her global conglomerate, is gonna make sure she is protected,” Cannon explained during a Nov. 9 appearance on the On Par with Maury Povich podcast. “Had no problem with it whatsoever. And when it was all said and done, I think it protected both of us.”

Although their divorce wasn’t finalized for two years following their 2014 breakup, the 46-year-old—dad to 10 additional children with five different women—said the process felt “easy.”

Nevertheless, it’s not a process Cannon sees himself going through again, as he doesn’t see himself ever getting remarried.

“I think because of some of the natures of, like, unmarrying are not good,” he continued. “And it’s just the process—you don’t realize it takes so long and how draining it is.”

And while divorce “didn’t affect” his friendship with Carey, he saw how divorce creates pressure points in relationships.

“It really could have affected how we handled one another as friends,” he admitted. “I saw the traps of how this could have turned really bad if we didn’t step in and really engage as a family.”

For Cannon, he considered the friendliness that exists between he and Carey an exception when it comes to divorce.

“We handled it so well,” he added. “We handled it out of love. Never heard me say a bad thing about her. Never heard her say a bad thing about me. And we just kind of made it about the kids.”

Meanwhile, Cannon—who is currently in a non-monogamous relationship with Bre Tiesi and Abby De La Rosa—previously went so far to admit that he and Carey might have another shot at romance in the future.

“We belong together,” he quipped to E! News in 2024, going on to say that he would “be stupid” not to get back together with Carey.

As for why the fantasy hasn’t turned into reality? Cannon acknowledged, “She don’t want me.”

Deadline Launches Its Sound & Screen Film Streaming Site

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Deadline on Tuesday launched the streaming hub for its Sound & Screen Film, its showcase of the music behind the awards season’s buzziest films and the artists who created it.

A total of 11 films from 13 studios, streamers and production companies took part in the event, one of Deadline’s most popular, which took place November 6 at UCLA’s Royce Hall. Backed by a full orchestra, composers and songwriters took to the stage to perform their scores and songs live, and discussed the evolution of their works.

This year’s composers included Daniel Pemberton, the composer of both blockbuster pic Project Hail Mary and Alex Gibney’s documentary Musk; Kris Bowers with The Eyes of Ghana; Siddhartha Khosla with the animated Swapped; Michael Abels for his South Korean sci-fi mashup Hope; Francesco Le Metre with Being Heumann; Rebekka Karijord with Tenzing; and the band Son Lux with its music for Your Mother Your Mother Your Mother. Related Stories News 'Being Heumann' Composer Francesco Le Metre Tapped Into Judy Heumann's Drive And A '70s Punk Rock Spirit - Sound & Screen Film Documentary Kris Bowers On Creating A Love Letter To Ghana & Film History With His Score For 'The Eyes Of Ghana' - Sound & Screen Film

As for songwriters, Diana Warren returned to the Sound & Screen stage to perform “One Right Here” from Maserati: The Brothers, a team-up of Andrea Bocelli and Jennifer Hudson; Marcus “Bellringer” Bell broke down the inspirations for his The Greatest song “Walk With Kings”; 4 Non Blondes frontwoman Linda Perry performed “What Lies With You” from the documentary Linda Perry: Let It Die Here; and David Byrne joined Pemberton to reveal how they came up with “Let Me Sell You a Dream,” the song that plays over the end credits of Musk. Watch on Deadline

The companies taking part in this year’s event: Amazon MGM Studios, Apple Original Films, Bleecker Street, Breakwater Studios, Higher Ground Productions, KTF Films, Magenta Light Studios, Mercury Studios, Netflix, Neon, Orion Pictures, Skydance Animation and Prime Video.

Next up on Deadline’s awards-season calendar is Contenders Los Angeles, its annual film-panel event with casts and creatives set to take place all day November 14 at the DGA Theater.

‘Maxton Hall’ Star Damian Hardung & ‘GOT’ Alum Charlotte Hope Set For Thriller ‘Human Remains’ — AFM

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Damian Hardung (Maxton Hall) and Charlotte Hope (Game Of Thrones) are set to star in crime-thriller Human Remains, which Blue Fox Entertainment is launching for sales at the AFM.

Based on the novel by Elizabeth Haynes, which is inspired by real events, William Nunez (The Laureate) is due to direct from a screenplay he co-wrote with Haynes.

Principal photography is scheduled to begin on location in Kent and around London in February 2027, with additional casting in process.

The synopsis reads: “Annabel (Hope) is a police analyst in a quiet English town who begins to notice a disturbing pattern: people are dying alone, their bodies going undiscovered for weeks, and the number of deaths is rising every month. Colin (Hardung) is an alluring yet unassuming man who insinuates himself into the lives of the lonely and forgotten, leaving nothing behind. While Annabel’s colleagues dismiss the deaths as tragic but natural, she suspects a serial killer is at work—and that he is already selecting his next victim: her. As the body count rises and the trail grows cold, Annabel must convince a department unwilling to listen and catch a killer no one can see before he strikes again.” Watch on Deadline Related Stories News Female-Led Action-Thriller 'Topple' Launches With Carla Diaz, Maria Pedraza, Tamara Feldman & Graham McTavish Among Cast -- AFM News 'Andy & Orson': First Look At Louis Partridge & Anson Boon In Coming-Of-Age Movie As Filming Wraps & Further Cast Revealed -- AFM

Pic will be produced by Douglas Waller and Nunez of Briarstone Pictures (The Conqueror: Hollywood Fallout) alongside Terri Dwyer (Bolan’s Shoes) and William Prince (Get Millie Black) of Level Six Films in the UK. Nunez also wrote the screenplay with Elizabeth Haynes.

Haynes, a former police intelligence analyst, drew on her professional experience in writing the novel. Her other works include the psychological thriller Into the Darkest Corner.

Executive producers for the film are James Huntsman and Lisa Gutberlet (Blue Fox Entertainment), Piers Holdsworth Hunt and Jan Pace (Analogue Entertainment), Michael Laundon and Michael Bassick (M2 Media Post), North End Pictures, (Martin McCabe, Tony Miller, and Mick Southworth, (Enriched Media), and Amer HIlal (Where to Land and Kubrick’s War).

“Elizabeth Haynes has created a thriller that is both deeply human and profoundly disturbing, exploring the loneliness and invisibility that can exist in modern society,” said director William Nunez. “Charlotte Hope brings extraordinary depth, vulnerability, and determination to Annabel, while Damian Hardung has the rare ability to be simultaneously charismatic and enigmatic, making him the perfect Colin. Together, they create a dynamic that is electric and emotionally gripping. At a time when audiences are looking for elevated thrillers with real substance, Human Remains feels especially relevant. I’m thrilled to be partnering again with Blue Fox Entertainment, whose passion for bold, commercially engaging storytelling makes them the ideal home for this project.”

“There’s a huge appetite worldwide for crime thrillers rooted in great IP, and Human Remains delivers on every level. It’s smart, unsettling and grounded in the same territory as Mare of Easttown and Zodiac,” added Lisa Gutberlet, President of Blue Fox Entertainment. “Charlotte Hope and Damian Hardung are a dynamic pairing with real global appeal, and we’re excited to bring this story to audiences around the world.”

Charlotte Hope is represented by Verve Talent and Literary Agency as well as United Agents in the UK. . Damian Hardung is represented by Subtitle Talent.

See Bruce Springsteen Finally Play Western Stars Songs in Public

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It’s been seven years since Bruce Springsteen released his acclaimed orchestral pop record Western Stars, but he’s never played any of the songs at a public concert despite all the touring he’s done in that time. That finally changed Monday evening when he performed his annual acoustic set at the Stand Up For Heroes benefit concert at New York City’s David Geffen Hall. The show included the Western Stars songs “Hello Sunshine” and “Moonlight Motel,” in addition to 2007’s “I’ll Work for You Love” and “Dancing in the Dark.”

In keeping with the grand tradition of Stand Up For Heroes, which is organized by the Bob Woodruff Foundation and has raised $123 million for veterans and their families over the past 20 years, Springsteen sprinkled dirty jokes throughout his set. (The show also included performances by Josh Johnson, Hasan Minhaj, the Resilient, Jon Stewart, and Alex Warren.)

“Guy goes into the library,” Springsteen deadpanned to the crowd. “Goes up to the librarian. He goes, ‘Ma’am, do you have a book on small penises?’ She goes, ‘I don’t think it’s in yet.’ ‘Yeah, that one.'”

He also revived the “voodoo penis” joke that he told at the 2018 Stand Up For Heroes event. It’s about a man who worries his wife will cheat on him while she’s out of town, so he buys her a “voodoo penis” that will magically go anywhere it’s asked.

“She realizes the husband didn’t tell her the word to turn off the voodoo penis,” Springsteen said. “After about 10 or 20 minutes, she’s had enough. She’s starting to get really nervous. ‘I gotta go to the hospital.’ She jumps in the car, hits the gas, she’s going 90 miles per hour in a 25-mile-per-hour zone. And she gets pulled over by a state trooper. He says, ‘License and registration.’ She says, ‘Officer, you don’t understand. A voodoo penis! A voodoo penis!’ He says, ‘Voodoo penis, my ass.'”

A joke about a sex shop worker selling a thermos to a woman requesting a plaid dildo was also familiar, though still delivered quite well. But the Western Stars songs were the clear highlight. In April 2019, Springsteen played the album in its entirety at a special concert in a barn on his New Jersey farm that was recorded for the Western Stars concert film and soundtrack. Last month, he sang “Stones,” “Hello Sunshine,” and “Moonlight Motel” during a private jam session at Danny Clinch’s Transparent Clinch Gallery in Asbury Park, New Jersey, on the same bill as Tom Morello, Dave Matthews, Jesse Malin, Daryl “DMC” McDaniels, and Taylor Momsen. But neither of those events was open to the public. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

In 2025, Springsteen explained to Rolling Stone why he’d held back from playing songs from the album at his arena and stadium shows. “If I was going to do that, I’d go out on probably a smaller tour, where I might engage an orchestra and play those records specifically,” he said. “When I go out with the E Street Band, I go out to do a rock show. I want to come out, put the pedal to the metal for three hours, and exhaust you, myself, and anybody in the immediate 20-mile vicinity. And so, there’s certain things that mix and certain things that don’t mix for me, and that’s sort of the way I look at the work that I do with the band.” Trending Stories Watch Ariana Grande, Ben Stiller, Robert De Niro Take Over ‘The Tonight Show’ Paul McCartney and Ringo Starr to Reunite at Las Vegas Show in 2027 Pink Becomes First Solo Female Pop Artist to Headline the Sphere Judge Sets Steven Tyler Sex Assault Trial Date

Stand Up For Heroes was the final event on Springsteen’s public calendar for 2026. But he is working on two separate solo albums. “I did finish one, and it is done,” he told Rolling Stone in September. “I’m in the process of working on this other one, which, because of the nature of the material, felt like it should come out first. So that’s the situation I’m in. I’m trying to finish that up, and I’d like to release that first. And then this other record that I made, which is not time-relevant, it can come out at any time and it’ll work.”

He’s not sure exactly when he’ll tour again. “Well, we toured quite a bit, and we toured the previous spring,” he said. “We toured this spring. And these days that’s what I like to do. I would like to go out and do 20, 30 shows. I don’t imagine a 140-show E Street tour will happen again. It’s just time is too short right now. But I do imagine a good series of simply shorter tours where we touch different places at different times. And the band is playing so well. In my opinion, if you saw this show, you saw one of the best shows we’ve ever done. The band is playing so well that we have a happy life there, and that’ll continue.”

Johnny Depp’s ‘Ebenezer’ Comeback: A Smaller $5 Million Salary, Optimistic Theater Owners and More

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Four years ago, Johnny Depp‘s fans and detractors descended on the sleepy town of Fairfax, Virginia, to cheer or jeer the actor in his ugly defamation case against ex-wife Amber Heard. Now, when local cinema operator Mark O’Meara probes his patrons about “Ebenezer,” a new take on “A Christmas Carol” featuring Depp as the coldhearted miser Scrooge, he’s met with decidedly less intensity about the embattled star’s first major movie role in nearly eight years.

“Some people can’t wait. They love Johnny Depp. And others are just like, ‘Meh,’” says O’Meara, who owns Fairfax’s University Mall and Cinema Arts theatres. “I don’t think it’s a polarizing thing.” Related Stories Raye Wins Mercury Prize for Album of the Year Raye Named Variety’s 2026 Hitmaker of the Year

“Ebenezer” isn’t expected to ignite the box office à la “Michael,” a musical biopic about a controversial figure. The Dickens adaptation, which opens on Nov. 13, is tracking a modest debut of about $20 million domestically and another $20 million overseas from 63 markets. But O’Meara doesn’t get the sense the public sees Depp as persona non grata, even though the actor’s career has been derailed by personal drama, including domestic abuse allegations leveled by Heard.

Paramount, meanwhile, is betting that moviegoers have been missing Depp, whose last Hollywood outing was 2018’s “Fantastic Beasts: The Crimes of Grindelwald.” (He was forced by Warner Bros. to exit the “Harry Potter” spinoff series in 2020 after losing a libel case against the British tabloid The Sun, which called the actor a “wife beater.” Depp won the subsequent defamation trial in the U.S.) An April 2026 study by the National Research Group suggests his fans never turned on him. Participants ranked Depp among the top 10 actors — ahead of Tom Hanks and behind Margot Robbie — who “would make you most interested in seeing a new movie in a cinema in the future.”

“Audiences don’t care. He’s still one of the biggest movie stars on the planet,” says Kevin Goetz, founder and CEO of audience research firm Screen Engine/ASI. “That, to me, is very telling about how often Hollywood perpetuates its own self-fulfilling prophecies.”

As Hollywood has kept a distance, Depp has remained a popular figure around the world, receiving career achievement and tribute awards from international film festivals and retaining a $20 million brand deal with Dior. Onscreen, he has appeared in underseen indies like the 2020 drama “Minamata” and the 2023 period piece “Jeanne du Barry,” and he directed the 2024 biopic “Modì, Three Days on the Wing of Madness.” And Depp’s fans have been vocal on social media, supporting the actor through his professional struggles and bizarrely rallying in 2021 to nominate “Minamata” for the Oscars first (and only) fan favorite award.

Lately, the town seems willing to forgive Depp. His next film is Lionsgate’s 2027 action thriller “Day Drinker,” co-starring Penélope Cruz. Meanwhile Disney is looking into “Ebenezer” and the potential backlash, before greenlighting another “Pirates of the Caribbean” movie, according to sources. Depp has said he’s open to returning to the franchise, but he noted “the only involvement I really want to have, truly, is just to send them off on a better note.”

“Ebenezer” doesn’t need to electrify the masses to justify its price tag. Given the uncertainty, Paramount cautiously spent $38 million to produce the Dickens tale. Depp was paid $5 million, a fraction of what he’s earned on prior studio movies, with the promise of bonuses tied to box office performance. Marketing expenses were around $50 million, including stops at San Diego Comic- Con where the actor made a surprise appearance, dressed in character, and threw handfuls of candy at the foot of attendees. (Paramount declined to comment on budgets.) Directed by Ti West, known for the “X” horror trilogy, “Ebenezer” spins the oft-told yarn of the curmudgeonly businessman who is visited by the ghosts of Christmas past, present and future.

“I’m optimistic for a few reasons,” says Regal’s CEO Eduardo Acuna. “Even though this story predates him, it feels like the character was written for Johnny Depp. And it’s good IP from a very distinctive filmmaker.”

Cinema operators like Acuna suggest the box office haul will speak less to Depp’s appeal and more to the movie’s quality. After all, there’s been no shortage of takes on “A Christmas Carol.” Scrooge has been a staple of holiday moviegoing for decades, with popular versions including the 1951 classic with Alastair Sim and 1988’s “Scrooged” with Bill Murray. The most recent entry, Disney’s 2009 “A Christmas Carol,” starring Jim Carrey, brought in $325 million against an overinflated $200 million budget.

“It’s a story that’s been told a number of times, so it’ll be more about whether this one is compelling,” says Classic Cinemas CEO Chris Johnson. “I don’t know if I’ve heard anyone saying they won’t see it because of Johnny Depp. The public seems to be pretty forgiving.”

For Depp, embodying a misanthrope like Scrooge — in the trailer, he’s seen scaring villagers and being stingy with Christmas bonuses — jibes with the other offbeat or unlikable characters he’s spun into the stuff of contemporary cinema classics. His real-life rebellious persona has helped sell his most famous roles, from the chaotic Captain Jack Sparrow from “Pirates of the Caribbean,” eccentric Willy Wonka from “Charlie and the Chocolate Factory” and the mercurial Mad Hatter in “Alice in Wonderland.”

“Johnny’s image was built on being a bad boy,” Goetz says. “He ain’t America’s dad. We don’t want him to be that.”

‘The Circle’ Officially Returns on Hulu With a Brand-New Trailer

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

When it comes to reality shows, there is one that captivates the world like no other. When The Circle originally premiered, it put a group of contestants in separate apartments, and each one had to create a social media profile. Left with the chat, the Circle, and their own thoughts, each person set out to become the most popular player in the game. The catch was that you weren't exactly sure if you were talking to a real person or a catfish. Now as the show moves to its new streaming home, Collider is thrilled to partner with Hulu to bring you a first look at the trailer for The Circle ​​​​​​before it returns next week.

Much like the show's original iteration, fans can expect a complex game of social strategy with unknown players competing against celebrities, along with entirely new twists as the series enters a completely new era on Hulu. This time, you can play along too. The new season begins on November 17, but Hulu is officially kicking off the competition tomorrow with the first fan vote of the season. For the first time, fans will have the power to vote and directly influence how the show plays out. The Circle is shot in real time, meaning the audience can vote and change the game. Voting opens at 6 a.m. PT on TikTok at @TheCircleHulu and via text; get all the details on how to cast your vote here.

In our exclusive new trailer, a lot of the setup for the show feels very similar. Contestants are in their own apartments; they have their own TVs where they can talk to the Circle and ask it to send messages for them, and some of the game's original rules appear to still be in play. You can get blocked, you have alerts, and there are group tasks to try to figure out who is who they say they are and who is not. The official logline for Hulu's new era of The Circle reads:

The Circle is back with a brand-new home on Hulu, reimagined as a fast-turnaround interactive event. For the first time, celebrities enter the ultimate game of popularity, playing alongside civilians, raising the stakes and the strategy like never before. Some players compete as themselves, while others are catfishing and doing whatever it takes to win. This time, America isn't just watching. Viewers have the power to stir the pot and influence the game in real time. This is a new era for the groundbreaking competition, where fame, strategy, and popularity collide in the biggest season of The Circle. yet.

Part of what made The Circle so much fun to watch was how the show allowed people to just exist on their own and see how they attempt to become "popular" without using in-person charms like most reality TV competitions. Watching people hang out by themselves and entertain themselves while stuck in an apartment without access to the outside world? Kind of hilarious television. Throwing celebrities into the mix more actively this time around is only going to give audiences even more reason to tune in.

The Circle is produced by Studio Lambert, together with Omaha Productions, Motion Entertainment, and in partnership with Turnkey Studio Services, a division of Banijay America. Executive producers for Studio Lambert include Tim Harcourt, Jack Burgess, Niall O’Driscoll and Stephen Lambert. Peyton Manning, Jamie Horowitz and Colin Campbell will executive produce for Omaha Productions and Martin Oxley and Chet Fenster for Motion Entertainment, a WPP Media company. Susan House serves as showrunner. The Circle is a Banijay Entertainment format.

Start voting tomorrow and don't miss the new era of The Circle when it premieres on Hulu on November 17.

Mormon Wives' Jen Affleck Says She's "Not Doing the Best" Amid Divorce

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Jen Affleck, who stars on Hulu’s upcoming Nov. 12 series Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County, shared insight into her priorities amid her and Zac Affleck’s divorce.

Jen Affleck is swinging into a new chapter.

After filing for divorce from husband Zac Affleck, the Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County star shared insight into how she’s navigating her new normal.

“I'm not doing the best right now,” Jen—who is currently pregnant with her and Zac’s fourth baby—admitted to E! News in an exclusive interview. “I'm just taking one day at a time, and really just focusing on my kids. And obviously, the timing sucks, especially with me being pregnant.”

Nevertheless, she affirmed, “Ultimately, it was the best decision for us.”

And with that right decision bolstering her sails, Jen enjoyed sailing into the new horizon alongside her Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County costars Bobbi Althoff, Aspyn Ovard, Avery Woods, Madison Bontempo, McCall DaPron, Salomé Andrea, Ashleigh Pease and Jordan McClanahan.

For the Dancing With the Stars alum, the timing of filming Orange County made to an experience that was more freeing than her seasons on Secret Lives of Mormon Wives.

“What's funny is there was no reinventing this time,” the 27-year-old admitted. “I just showed up as me. I feel like for the past seven, eight years of my life, I've tried to mold myself into who I think people want to see, whether it be for my marriage or for TV or for the world. And this year was the first time that I really felt like I was reclaiming my identity and who I actually am.”

So, “going into the season, I just poured my heart into it,” she added. “Nothing was storyline; everything was real.”

That meant allowing the cameras to track her difficult chapter as it played out.

“I'm obviously not excited for people to see all the painful parts of the show,” she continued, “but I'm looking forward to people seeing the real, real me—and I guess I'm still trying to figure out who the real me is as well. So, we'll see that unfold in the future.”

As for how Jen showed up to The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County, fans can tune in for the premiere on Thursday, November 12 on Hulu and Hulu on Disney+.

While Jen has in large part remained publicly silent on her divorce—though she did share two videos from her and Zac’s relationship, highlighting his less-than-ideal behavior—Zac shared a five-minute video sharing insight into their split.

“This is something we've been talking about for a long time,” he said of their breakup in the Nov. 5 TikTok. “Jen came to me about a year ago, after Dancing With the Stars, and told me she was unhappy and wanted a divorce. And I basically begged her to stay, told her I’d work on things, fix things, become whoever she needed me to be."

It was during this time that Jen became pregnant with their fourth baby, who will join siblings Nora, 5, Luca, 3, and Penelope, 16 months.

And, much like Jen, Zac wants to make sure their children remain his and her priority.

"That's really my priorities," he shared. "I feel like probably the thing that's eating me alive the most is I feel like I failed my kids, and I'm worried that I'm going to cause them damage. And I just hope that we can still give them a normal childhood, but as far as that goes, Jen and I are definitely on the same page of wanting to give them the best the best life we can."

As for why their marriage came to an end, he reflected, “Marriage is hard. Having your marriage in the public is hard. Raising kids is hard. And I think slowly over time we lost that spark."

Meanwhile, for a closer look at where Jen and Zac’s SLOMW: OC costars stand in their love lives, read on. And don’t miss the premiere of The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County on Nov. 12.

Following her divorce from Cory Althoff, podcaster Bobbi Althoff is dating Tyler Hawkins, who plays a special role in Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County.

"He styles me, that’s how he supports me,” she told E! News in May, adding that the basketball player, "picks out my outfits every day. He styles me every day for the show. It’s great."

Aspyn Ovard, who filed for divorce from Parker Ferris in 2024 after eight years of marriage, is currently in a throuple relationship with married couple Billy Davis and Bri Davis.

After many ups and downs, Jen Affleck—who is pregnant with her and Zac Affleck's fourth baby—officially filed for divorce from her husband of seven years, E! News confirmed Nov. 4.

Despite ongoing relationship speculation, Salomé Andrea and Aaron Brimhall are still going strong as they prepare to welcome their third baby together. The duo, who married in 2012, also share 8-year-old twins Luca and Rue.

McCall DaPron—the sister of Mormon Wives' Mayci Neeley—shares four kids with husband Kirk DaPron, who she wed in 2011.

Ashleigh Pease and Nate Pease are rock solid after over a decade of marriage. "This 13th year together is one that has felt full circle!" she wrote on Instagram in September. "A season of fruits. Comfort. The happiest milestones."

Madison Bontempo and Kyler Steven Fisher, who wed in 2013, have been open about the highs and lows of their relationship. "Kyler and I aren’t perfect," she wrote on Instagram in October. "We’ve had arguments. We’ve had hard seasons. We’ve had moments we wish we could take back. And we’re not afraid to admit that."

But, she added, "Those moments aren’t our whole story. There’s also laughter, friendship, forgiveness, growth, five beautiful children, a million memories, and a love that has continued to evolve through every season of our lives."

Avery Woods and David Woods, parents to Ziggy and Stevie, are still feeling the love after a decade of marriage. As she simply put it, "my forever + always."

Bill Hader Has Highbrow Taste in Children’s Movies

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

You can trust Bill Hader on movies. From gushing about John Ford and Akira Kurosawa in the Criterion Closet, to spoofing Errol Morris and D.A. Pennebaker in Documentary Now!, to creating, directing and starring in four seasons of HBO’s Barry—the SNL alum’s cinematic expertise is well-documented. Hader knows a thing or two about kids’ movies in particular, having lent his iconic voice to animated tentpoles like Inside Out and Cloudy With a Chance of Meatballs. This week, you can hear Hader in The Cat in The Hat, a new adaptation of the 1957 Dr. Seuss classic, in which he voices the titular troublemaking feline.

Written and directed by Alessandro Carloni and Erica Rivinoja, this new iteration of the beloved children’s story is an inviting romp that is mischievous enough for kids, grounded enough for grown-ups, and teeming with gags and good nature that all can enjoy. The film is an ideal showcase for Hader’s gymnastic vocal talents, and also boasts Quinta Brunson, Matt Berry, and Giancarlo Esposito among its cast. (It’s a more family-friendly—though no less amusing—take on the Cat than when Hader last played the character, in a 2014 SNL sketch.)

A father to three daughters, Hader has seen it all—from Frozen and Tangled to Off Campus—and does his part, when possible, to pass down the classic films that were formative during his own childhood. “You don’t want to push it too hard,” he tells GQ on a recent call. “For every one I show them, there’s 10 that I start and they go, ‘Ugh, Dad, this is boring.’” The rest of us, however, are eager to take note. Below, Hader shares some of his favorite quote-unquote “kids’ movies” whose cinematic merit makes them a must-see at any age.

“When I was young, my dad told me that was a movie that he watched as a kid. I didn't grow up in a time when there were a lot of Westerns, but [for] my dad and uncles, that was all that was on TV.

What I was moved by was the relationship [between] Alan Ladd as Shane and this little boy [Brandon deWilde] enamored with a gunfighter. It has this heroic sensibility to it, but underneath was this honesty of, ‘You don't want that life,’ which I thought was interesting. To me, Van Heflin, the dad, was the cooler guy. So it upended those expectations of what the movie was going to be.

It has this ending where it's poignant and kind of sad, but I remember thinking, ‘Oh, that's good that that's what happens at the end of the story.’ It's a good coming-of-age story… It's kind of sneaky. You could see it on the face of it just being this cut-and-dry, Western-hero type of movie. But when you really think about it, even as a kid, I got a sense that there's a lot of depth to it.”

“Buster Keaton is one of my favorites, and that was one I showed my kids. I have three daughters. Now they're 17, 14, and 12, but at the time, they were very little. I'm a movie nerd, so I was gonna show them some silent movies, and I was like, ‘I know. Everybody's gonna roll their eyes at me for showing the kids silent movies. How pretentious.’ But they really responded to Buster Keaton, specifically Sherlock Jr. and Three Ages, his take on the movie Intolerance by D.W. Griffith. They just loved it, and it was fun getting to watch it through their eyes.

I recently took my youngest [to see] it at the Aero Theater in LA, and it still was great. To see a movie that was made over a hundred years ago still work on all [levels], like… it works better than most movies now. It's just very simple. [Keaton] wasn't some kind of auteur guy. He was a vaudeville comedian that was curious about filmmaking; what you could do with editing, what you could do with shots. And I think he ended up making the blueprint of how you shoot physical comedy and action. It’s pretty amazing.

What's interesting about some of those silent movies is that they don't really know what they're doing. The best stuff comes out of, ‘I don't know, I had this idea, let's figure it out in a really open, curious way.’ And in doing that, you invent the language.

Charlie Chaplin, too. [I’ve shown] them City Lights and The Circus and The Gold Rush. But they were really drawn to Buster Keaton, I think, because he was kind of dry and you didn't really know what he was thinking, and so you could put all your feelings onto him.”

“That's one that my parents showed me when I was a kid. It's a long movie, but it has so many crazy [moments] and a lot of physical comedy. I just love all those comedians in the movie. Every time someone pulls up, you're like, ‘Oh my God, it's Jonathan Winters. I love Phil Silvers. Oh my God, Spencer Tracy's in this movie!’ Dick Shawn is my personal favorite in that movie, the guy who's the real mama's boy.

It's kind of the one Stanley Kramer movie I like. Stanley Kramer movies tended to be, I don't know, a little sentimental, or overtly didactic or something, [but] this movie is just having a ton of fun. I have very fond memories of watching it with my parents and my grandparents. My dad was in town recently, and when I came home, he was watching it with my kids, and they were all laughing really hard and telling me scenes from it.

They really loved it, but [just like] with the silent movies, you don't want to push it too hard. For every [classic movie] I show them, there's 10 that I start and they go, ‘Ugh, Dad, this is boring,’ and I go, ‘Alright, we can watch Nobody Wants This or Off Campus.’”

“The Secret of NIMH was one that I loved as a kid, because it was ostensibly a kid's movie, but it was incredibly dark and disturbing. It has stuff that I still find really frightening. And I loved the mythology of it. As a kid, I liked the idea that in your backyard [there] could be this whole kingdom that you don't even know about.

There's specifically a shot in it with the crow, played by Dom DeLuise, and in the distance they see this cat. There's this long shot [of] this field, and then suddenly, just really quickly, you see this cat pop up and go back down under [as] it's trying to catch a bird or something. That shot had a huge effect on me. I thought, ‘Oh man, that's so terrifying.’

And then the story of the rats and NIMH, and they're all being tested on, and they fall into this abyss, and the whole ending where they're having this sword fight… I just thought it was fantastic. Everything was high stakes. And it wasn't Disney. I liked Disney, but Disney never went that dark.

Maybe not a lot of people agree with me, [but] I’m definitely someone [that] likes when something's scary for a kid, within reason. A lot of people are like, ‘No, no, no, they can't see that.’ And it's like, ‘No, let them see it. Get scared. That's good.’ I was happy that I was scared.

I'm sure if The Secret of NIMH came out now, they would have a whole room of executives going, ‘You can't have the owl with its head upside-down, turning around and with cobwebs all over him. It's just too scary.’ But for me, it scared me to death, and then I would keep watching it, and then it became less scary, and then I started to appreciate it. I think it's good for kids to be scared.”

“That movie came out when I was a freshman in high school. I got a job on the school paper, and my first job was to write a review of all the new movies, and it was the week that that movie came out. And so I remember I went with a notebook, I was writing my thoughts down, and then I ended up writing a pretty bad [review]. I liked [the movie] a lot, but my actual writing was terrible. The editors took it and rewrote it all.

But I loved it. I love Tim Burton. I loved the music in it, specifically the song that Catherine O'Hara sings as Sally; I think it's such a moving song. And Henry Selick’s direction in it is amazing. People always think Tim Burton made that movie. He didn't. It's Henry Selick. He spent every day there with those animators making that thing work.

I love the scope of it and how grand it is, from the beginning—that ‘This Is Halloween’ song, there's something almost operatic about it. And it's its own thing. The best stuff, to me, is always when the filmmakers and the writers [are] doing a thing that they want to see, and they trust an audience will get it.

When it came out, I remember it wasn't what it is now. It was kind of this weird oddity. And then as time went on… I remember moving to LA, and it was the 10-year anniversary of it, and they had a screening someplace, and I drove past all these people dressed like the characters, they had Jack Skellington t-shirts on and all that stuff, and I went, ‘Wow, that's crazy.’ Cause I was a huge fan of it, and had the soundtrack, and I absolutely adored that movie.

[Once] I had kids, it was one of the first movies I showed them, and they watched it every Halloween and every Christmas. My youngest daughter [recently] played Sally in a school play, and she got to sing that song, and so it was full circle. I got very emotional. I was like, ‘Oh my gosh, there she is, singing that song.’ And I had become friendly with Catherine O'Hara, and she passed away, and it was really… Yeah, I was a blubbering idiot when she started singing that song.”

Oscar Contender ‘Bucks Harbor’ Set To Begin Awards-Qualifying Run; Watch Clip From Pete Muller’s Impressive Directorial Debut

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Bucks Harbor, one of the most acclaimed documentaries of the year, is about to begin its Oscar-qualifying theatrical run.

The film directed by Pete Muller opens at Firehouse: DCTV’s Cinema for Documentary Film in Manhattan on December 4. The feature documentary set in a remote coastal village of Northern Maine, where generations of men have stoically fished the waters in an often-hazardous pursuit of a living, premiered in February at the Berlin Film Festival. At the Berlinale, it won the Prize of the Ecumenical Jury and the Audience Award in the festival’s Panorama section. Bucks Harbor also won Best International Documentary Film at the Zurich Film Festival, Best Documentary Film at the Tacoma Film Festival in Washington State, the Audience Award at the Margaret Mead Film Festival in New York, and the Fowlie Audience Award at the Camden International Film Festival in Maine, among many other awards. Related Stories Casting Millie Bobby Brown To Star In, Produce Netflix Comedy 'Non-Refundable'; Jack Innanen Also Set News Female-Led Action-Thriller 'Topple' Launches With Carla Diaz, Maria Pedraza, Tamara Feldman & Graham McTavish Among Cast -- AFM

Bucks Harbor screens at DOC NYC on Sunday after being named to that festival’s Winners Circle for 2026. Watch an exclusive clip from the film below.

“Set against the rugged beauty of Downeast Maine, the film portrays a lobster fishing community shaped by the unforgiving coastal landscape,” notes a synopsis. “As Bucks Harbor delicately explores working class manhood, kinship and the act of listening, it situates human life alongside the ancient creatures that sustain it, offering a revelatory, searing and ultimately hopeful portrait.”

The documentary features several generations of indelible individuals in Bucks Harbor: David Cale, Vicki Patchell, Wayne Robinson, Mark Alley, Sharon Alley, Larson Alley, Mike Murphy, Kirby Murphy, Carter Murphy, Ryan Murphy, and Isaac Reynolds.

Muller, who makes his directorial debut with Bucks Harbor, won an Emmy as co-producer of The First Wave. He’s an award-winning photographer who spent more than a decade documenting conflict in post-colonial societies around the world. Muller developed his interest in photography as a youth after accompanying his mother while she worked night shifts as a newspaper photographer in Lynn, MA.

“[W]e’d race to fires, shootings, and other emergencies that crackled through the police scanner,” Muller writes in a director’s statement. “From the safety of the company car, where she insisted I remain, I’d watch her move through the flashing red and blue lights of bad situations. At the end of the night, in the darkroom, I’d ask questions about what we’d seen and why. Her answers were shaped by compassion and became the basis of my education about the world.

“I nourished my interest in people by studying history but learned I wasn’t built to sit quietly and read books. Visual journalism seemed a better fit as it rewarded my desire to be so close that I’d be shoved and splattered. For nearly 15 years, I worked as a photojournalist in Africa and the Middle East, seeking to understand and communicate the causes of armed conflict. During that time, I came to see ideas of masculinity as a central feature of the conflicts I witnessed.”

Muller continues, “In 2021, I returned to New England and began what would become Bucks Harbor. I spent the next five years in winding, intimate conversations with the film’s participants, who came to feel like extended family. Each felt deeply familiar, reminding me of relatives, close friends, and myself. While Bucks Harbor is built around their lives, it reflects mine, too.

“I’ve long interrogated the lessons we were taught as boys: be tough and strong, don’t accept humiliation, don’t express fear or insecurity. What did those lessons prepare us for? What do our families, friends, and societies truly want and need from us? And most importantly, how do we feel beneath the stoic exteriors we were encouraged to assume?”

Muller adds that his documentary “is not solely an exploration of men and masculinity. While men and boys offer central perspectives, women contribute vital, illuminating insights along the way. The film is about survival, love, and the emotional armor we all develop. It’s also about the experience of working people in the United States and the quiet courage and fortitude required just to get by. And it explores our abiding relationship with the natural world and the wisdom it offers if we’re willing to listen. I hope Bucks Harbor can serve as a constructive, humanistic tile in the mosaic of our social conversation and reflect my fundamental belief that we need each other. The more we can relate and empathize, the closer we might move toward shared understanding.”

Bucks Harbor is directed and produced by Pete Muller and produced by Nathan Golon and Noel Paul. The cinematography is by Nathan Golon and Pete Muller. Lobster cinematography is by Mark Unger, Anand Varma, and Pete Muller. Noel Paul edited the film. Nikolaj Hess composed the original score; the film also features an original composition by Hiss Golden Messenger. Executive producers are Kaitlin Yarnall and Greg Moga.

In the exclusive clip below, Bucks Harbor’s David Cale fishes from a dock with his cousin’s son, Isaac Reynolds.

‘I Play Rocky’ writer says rival studios balked at ‘advertising another studio’s franchise’

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sylvester Stallone first played Rocky Balboa 50 years ago this month. Now Anthony Ippolito is playing Stallone in I Play Rocky, a movie about the making of the original that began life without the studio that owns the franchise.

Directed by Peter Farrelly, the film follows Stallone’s hardscrabble push to write Rocky and his insistence on playing the title role himself. It premiered at the Toronto International Film Festival in September, where it was first runner-up for the People’s Choice Award.

It is also the first film from the Rocky universe to come together entirely under Amazon’s ownership, giving Amazon MGM Studios a fresh way to introduce one of its marquee franchises to a new generation. According to screenwriter Peter Gamble, that payoff is exactly why rival studios passed on it.

Rocky opened in November 1976, earned 10 Academy Award nominations, and won three, including Best Picture. It grossed about $117 million at the domestic box office. Along with The Godfather, Jaws, and Star Wars, it is often credited with kicking off Hollywood’s blockbuster era of entertaining, grandiose, myth-affirming movies that sparked billion-dollar franchises.

Five sequels followed between 1979 and 2006, then the Creed spinoff trilogy, plus merchandise from action figures to mobile games. For a roughly $1 million movie about a fighter going the distance, Rocky has stayed relevant across generations: the nine films have grossed about $948 million domestically and $1.9 billion worldwide, according to The Numbers.

That library is a big part of what Amazon bought when it agreed to pay $8.45 billion for MGM, home to both Rocky and James Bond. The deal closed in 2022, and MGM now sits inside Amazon MGM Studios. Its parent has since become No. 1 on the 2026 Fortune 500, ending Walmart’s 13-year run at the top.

The franchise has already paid off once for its new owner. Creed III opened to $58.7 million in March 2023, and it held the record for Amazon’s best opening since the MGM deal until Project Hail Mary topped it this year. But Creed III was already in production when the acquisition closed.

I Play Rocky was developed without the studio. Gamble wrote the script on spec; it landed on the Black List, then reached producers Christian Baha and Toby Emmerich and director Farrelly. In 2024, they took it to the Marché du Film at Cannes to court financiers, choosing the independent route over studio financing.

“Technically you can tell a story about Stallone because he’s a public figure, and technically you can show scenes from the film as long as you comment on the scenes,” Gamble told Fortune. “So in theory, you don’t need the rights to Rocky to make this movie, but just because something is technically true doesn’t mean that it’s the smartest thing to do.”

Dramatizing the making of Rocky might have been protected as fair use, but some of the original’s iconography, such as Bill Conti’s unmistakable theme, “Gonna Fly Now,” would have been difficult and expensive to include without MGM’s cooperation. Copyright law is not an exact science, and every reference to the original film would have put the production on a treacherous line.

Distribution posed the same problem, since a movie about the origins of one studio’s franchise was a hard sell to everyone else. “We tried to go to the different studios, but the studios had the problem of ‘If we make this story, it’s going to be advertising another studio’s franchise,'” Gamble explained. “They were kind of like, ‘We don’t know if that’s really the risk we want to take because a lot of the reward goes to someone else. This is really an Amazon MGM project.'”

Amazon MGM signed on to distribute the film in 2025, before cameras rolled, which cleared I Play Rocky to borrow from the franchise. The finished film uses Conti’s theme and recreates exact shots from the original.

If it works, the film delivers exactly what rival studios feared: a new generation of Rocky fans for Amazon. It has been nearly 20 years since the last Rocky movie, 2006’s Rocky Balboa, and 11 since Creed hit theaters.

The early numbers are encouraging. I Play Rocky opened on seven screens on Nov. 6 and grossed about $345,000, roughly $49,300 per screen, according to The Numbers. It goes wide on Nov. 20, as Rocky enters its sixth decade.

BMG’s Concord Originals Preps Supernatural Gothic Horror Pic ‘Wolf at the Door’ (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The five-year-old division is taking a 360-degree approach to an expanded portfolio following Concord's merger with music and theater powerhouse BMG and acquisition of RKO's classic film library

The full moon is out at Concord Originals, the film and TV arm of music and theater powerhouse BMG, where it is developing and producing supernatural gothic horror feature “Wolf at the Door.”

Based on the eponymous play from Concord Theatricals, the genre pic will be helmed by Argentina’s Agustina San Martín, who recently directed the psychological horror feature “Psyche,” starring Noah Jupe (“A Quiet Place”) and Isabela Merced (“The Last of Us”), currently in post. Related Stories Olivia Rodrigo, Katseye and Sienna Spiro to Be Honored at Variety’s 10th Annual Hitmakers Event Raye Named Variety’s 2026 Hitmaker of the Year

The adaptation is written by playwright, poet and screenwriter Marisela Treviño Orta, based on her play “Wolf at the Door,” part of a trilogy of theatrical productions inspired by Latinx mythology and the Brothers Grimm fairy tales.

Set in 19th century New Mexico, it centers on a young woman, close to giving birth to her first child, who foretells the return of a malevolent half-man half-wolf creature, only to discover that another predator has already entrenched itself in her hacienda. Casting is underway.

“Wolf at the Door” is produced by Concord Originals’ Emmy-nominated exec VP Sophia Dilley (HBO’s “Stax: Soulsville, USA”) and VP of development Charles Hopkins (“Billy Preston: That’s the Way God Planned It”). Concord Originals’ Quíle Miguel Gomez, Victor Zaraya and Bob Valentine executive produce.

“We’re film executives within a music and theater company, and this is the first play we’ve commissioned and developed entirely in-house. I’m really excited about what this represents and how it demonstrates our team’s ability to go beyond simply packaging and building projects,” said Dilley.

Hopkins concurred: “This project encapsulates exactly what Concord Originals is all about: identifying world-class IP under the BMG roof and reimagining it for screen.”

The eight-person team of this relatively young division, founded in 2021, has its work cut out for it, given the deep vaults of BMG’s catalog plus those of Concord Theatricals (18,000+) and the recent acquisition of RKO, which adds another 5,000+ IP assets, including such classic cinema titles as Alfred Hitchcock’s “Suspicion” and Fritz Lang’s “The Woman in the Window.”

“RKO has such a rich history and so much of it hasn’t been oversaturated, which makes it ripe for reinvention and a chance to reach new generations. It also feels like a deep well of stories that’s particularly timely for where the industry is today. Historically, RKO was interesting in how it made films on contained budgets, and I’m excited to explore what that approach could look like today,” Dilley remarked.

Concord Originals’ scripted slate also includes “Bleeding Love,” an original movie musical featuring hits from multi Grammy-winner Ryan Tedder, a limited series adaptation of Rodgers & Hammerstein’s “Cinderella” with Skydance TV and projects with Jennifer Lopez’s Nuyorican Prods. for Disney+.

Reflecting on the fact that “Wolf” director San Martin is a protégé of BMG client Lin-Manuel Miranda for the Rolex Mentor and Protégé Arts Initiative, Hopkins said: “That is the advantage we now have in this bigger organization, is that there are these connecting dots between all of the talent that we work with on the filmmaking side as well as the music and theater side. So I think we’ll encounter more and more of those common connections among the artists we work with in the future.”

Dilley pointed out that a single property could generate a film, stage production, album and other associated content.

“We cover so many different areas, so the exciting part is bringing them together. Our ambition is to take a 360 approach to projects wherever we can, connecting film and TV with music and theater … bringing artists into a series, playwrights into the writing process, or developing a film that also has a stage production and an accompanying album,” said Dilley.

“We have the opportunity to be involved in the life cycle of these copyrights, from beginning to end. Ultimately, it’s about keeping artists, iconic legacies and great IP in the zeitgeist, championing them and giving them the biggest cultural impact possible. That’s what guides everything we do.”

‘Wildwood’ Officially Faces an Uphill Box Office Battle

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Animated movies have been as popular as ever in 2026, with the biggest hitters unsurprisingly hailing from major franchises. The animated year's first big hit was The Super Mario Galaxy Movie, a video game adaptation sequel starring Chris Pratt, Charlie Day, Anya Taylor-Joy, and Jack Black. The film became the first billion-dollar hit in the best year since the COVID-19 pandemic in theaters, setting the tone for what would come a few months later.

Animation fans across the world would find their second billion-dollar success in June, as the return of Woody (Tom Hanks), Buzz (Tim Allen), Jessie (Joan Cusack), and the rest of the gang in Pixar's Toy Story 5 returned more than $1.1 billion at the box office, against a reported production budget of $250 million. But the all-conquering success of major franchise additions won't satisfy the hearts of animation purists, and the latest box office weekend produced yet another blow.

The geniuses at Laika returned with another stop-motion masterpiece this Fall in the form of Wildwood. However, despite earning some of the best reviews of the year and quickly becoming the heir apparent to the Best Animated Feature crown at next year's Oscars, it's been anything but a smooth ride for Wildwood in theaters. Last weekend, the fantasy favorite took a 22% hit in ticket sales, earning just $7 million domestically and taking its global total to $47.5 million. This is infuriating for anyone who has campaigned for people to watch Wildwood, which is reported to have a $100 million budget and continues to look unlikely to succeed financially.

Called "Laika's masterpiece" in Aidan Kelley's 9/10 review for Collider, Wildwood is earning some of the best reviews of the year. Director Travis Knight's latest passion project benefits from a superb voice cast, led by Peyton Elizabeth Lee as Prue McKeel, who ventures into a dangerous woodland in search of her kidnapped little brother. The voice cast also features Jacob Tremblay, Carey Mulligan, Angela Bassett, Richard E. Grant, Amandla Stenberg, Awkwafina, and two-time Academy Award winner Mahershala Ali.

Wildwood is available to watch now in theaters. Make sure to stay tuned to Collider for more of the latest box office stories.

James Gunn's Justice League? New Details Emerge About His Final DC Universe Movie

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The hierarchy of power in the DC Universe is changing — again. James Gunn and Peter Safran are out at DC Studios, with Marvel Studios veteran Jeremy Latcham taking their place. It's pretty shocking news, considering Gunn and Safran have only been on the job four years. Moreover, it was only a few weeks ago that they indicated David Ellison was in favor of keeping them on following the butchering of Warner Bros. and Paramount to form his new mega-studio Skydance.

For the moment, it's unclear whether the DCU as it currently exists (itself merely the latest overhaul of DC's shared movie and television universe in the last decade or so) will be rebooted once again, much less what will happen with the DCU projects being actively developed. We know Gunn's "Man of Tomorrow" is set to come out next year (although it's not as though WB is known for being above shelving finished films!), yet projects like the recently announced "Poison Ivy" TV show are a whole other matter.

Now, blogger Jeff Sneider has reported that Gunn was writing a third "Superman" movie before his exit from DC Studios. However, the film has (per Sneider) "since morphed into a 'Justice League' movie (possibly a two-parter) that could introduce Batman, whom Gunn had originally hoped to introduce in 'The Brave and the Bold.'"

Gunn released a statement shortly after the news of his exit from DC Studios came out, explaining that he is ready to be a "pure" filmmaker again and no longer have to deal with the politics of being an executive. Regardless of whether that's entirely true or not, the fact that Gunn has been closely involved with every DC Studios project released or greenlit so far indicates that he has indeed been keeping rather busy running the DCU.

If the report is accurate and James Gunn's final DC Studios project is now a "Justice League" movie, then that doesn't bode well. After all, the last time DC pivoted from a Superman sequel to suddenly introducing Batman and the rest of the League, it sent the DC Extended Universe careening off the rails.

And yet, given how much care Gunn has put into his DCU, it would make sense that he would want to bring in as many DC heavy hitters as he can before leaving. And assuming he actually stays on to write and direct this third and final DCU project of his, Gunn would also be able to establish the casting and tone of the DCU's Batman rather than entrusting that to Jeremy Latcham.

/Film's own Ryan Scott has speculated about why Skydance would want Gunn out of DC Studios, and one possible explanation that sticks out is just how slow Gunn and Peter Safran have been in bringing out the A-list DC characters. So far, the DCU has instead focused on showcasing the diversity of stories it can support, from the weird to the inspiring and the horrifying. After all, everyone already knows Batman exists in the DCU, but now they're aware it's home to Clayface and the Creature Commandos as well thanks to Gunn and Safran.

This approach has, in turn, allowed DC Studios to give filmmakers the time and care to ensure they don't rush their projects. Unfortunately, with the sheer amount of debt Skydance now has due to its absurd and unnecessary Warner Bros./Paramount merger, it's not shocking that it wants to rush into "Justice League" (despite knowing how badly that went last time).

Christopher Meloni Officially Returns to ‘Law & Order: SVU’ This Week for Episode 600

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Hannah covers horror, sci-fi, and games at Collider, where she writes news and features, reviews new releases, and interviews the people who make them. She’s helping build out Collider’s gaming coverage, with Pokémon as one of her specialties. If she isn’t writing about a monster, she’s probably watching one on a tape from her VHS collection.

The last time Olivia Benson and Elliot Stabler saw each other on Law & Order: Special Victims Unit, they were saying goodbye to Captain Donald Cragen. Then Stabler tossed out a casual “Love you” as he left, giving fans something else to talk about after the wake. That was the Season 27 premiere, back in September 2025. This week, Benson has a much harder conversation waiting for him.

Benson and Stabler spent the first 12 seasons of SVU working together. Back then, both of them answered to Cragen. Now Benson is the captain, and she’s the one telling her former partner that they need to take another look at a conviction from the beginning of their careers. In NBC’s promo, she’s already past wondering whether they made a mistake. “This is one of our first cases, and we got it wrong,” she tells him.

Christopher Meloni returns as Elliot Stabler in the 600th episode of Law & Order: SVU, airing Thursday, November 12, at 9 p.m. ET on NBC. Titled “Obedience,” the Season 28 episode reunites Meloni with Mariska Hargitay as the squad follows new leads in one of Benson’s earliest cases. It’s also Meloni’s first appearance on SVU since Law & Order: Organized Crime was canceled after five seasons.

The Rookie started as a show about a man starting over at forty-five and turned into a proper ensemble drama somewhere around season three. Here is which of them you are, and the case for yours being the best of the four.

You started late and you are not embarrassed about it. Nolan walks into the academy at forty-five surrounded by people half his age, and the show is honest that this is both his biggest disadvantage and the only reason he is any good at the job.

You are better at this than people expect and you intend to keep proving it. Chen goes from rookie to the best undercover officer in the division, which works because she is genuinely good at becoming somebody else and genuinely bad at pretending she does not care.

You are hard on people because you have decided that is what caring looks like. Bradford is introduced as the worst training officer in the building and slowly revealed as the one who takes the job most seriously, which are the same fact seen from two angles.

You said what you wanted and then you went and got it. Lopez wanted detective from the first episode, said so out loud, and got there by being better at the work than the people who were quieter about wanting it.

An old conviction gives Benson a reason to call the one person who knows what happened in the room when those early decisions were made. The trouble is that Stabler helped make them. It’s a good use of the reunion because the trust they built as partners cuts both ways. Benson knows she can ask him to come back and help. She also has to ask him to question conclusions they once trusted each other to reach. Meanwhile, it’s her squad that has to follow the new evidence wherever it goes. For the detectives following those leads, that means putting their own captain’s work under the microscope.

The episode also has a second reunion tucked into it. Laura Innes, who played Dr. Kerry Weaver on ER, guest stars opposite Hargitay, who had a recurring role on the medical drama before SVU. William Sadler also guest stars, marking his second appearance on the series after 2019’s “Facing Demons.”

Law & Order: SVU’s 600th episode airs Thursday, November 12, at 9 p.m. ET on NBC and streams Friday, November 13, on Peacock.

Alexander Skarsgard to Narrate Quinn Audio Erotica Original Set in World of ‘Wicker’

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The story won a fan-fiction contest launched by the app and film ahead of the latter's theatrical release.

The Wicker phenomenon is now venturing into audio erotica.

Wicker star Alexander Skarsgard will narrate Felled, the winning entry in a fan-fiction contest inspired by the world of Black Bear’s hit film, for audio erotica app Quinn, it was announced Tuesday.

Last month, ahead of the film’s theatrical release, Quinn partnered with Wicker to launch a fan-fiction contest inviting fans to create original stories set in the world of the film. With hundreds of submissions from around the world, Emma Parkinson’s Felled was selected as the winning entry. Related Stories Movies 'Godzilla Minus Zero' Powers $31M in Historic Box Office Launch Against Subdued 'Cat in the Hat' Movies 'You Can See Everything,' 'Clayface' Fighting for No. 1 in Quiet Halloween Weekend

The story follows a willow tree that forms a deep attachment to a farmer’s daughter before being cut down. When they reunite years later, they discover that their bond has endured.

“I haven’t really experienced this before, where I’ve been part of a project that has taken on a life of its own like this,“ Skarsgard said in a statement. “It’s also been really fun and inspiring to see how creative people are.”

When asked what drew him to collaborate with Quinn, Skarsgard said, “It was quite refreshing to have something sensual and erotic that wasn’t androcentric for a change!”

“Wicker fans have been so passionate about the film since day one, so partnering was a no-brainer,” says Caroline Spiegel, founder and CEO of Quinn. “There were hundreds of incredible, creative stories about mermen, porcelain men, witches and more. Felled stood out, and we knew our users had to hear Alexander narrate it.”

From directors, co-writers and real-life married couple Eleanor Wilson and Alex Huston Fischer, Black Bear’s Wicker has taken on a life of its own, going viral on social media and becoming an erotic, meme-making phenomenon.

The film, released on Oct. 23 and based on author Ursula Wills-Jones’s short story The Wicker Husband, follows “an ostracized fisherwoman (Olivia Colman) who commissions a husband made of wicker (Skarsgard) from the local basketmaker (Peter Dinklage). When her new husband awakens her desires and captures the village’s attention, jealousy, fascination and chaos follow, challenging the conventions of marriage, sex and the rules that bind them.“

“We always thought of the sex scenes as the comedy of the film, too,“ Wilson told THR. “They were supposed to be played for laughs, and they are. But I think once we actually had Alexander in costume, Olivia and Ita O’Brien, our intimacy coordinator, working together on these scenes — that’s when they did become surprisingly more sexy than we thought they would.“

The film even features a full frontal look but only when the Colman’s fisherwoman is looking at the nude wicker man off in the distance in the water.

Felled is the latest Quinn original series to be added to the company’s growing catalog of produced romances that have enlisted Hollywood talent. Other stars who have lent their voices to Quinn originals include Wilson Bethel, Chris Briney, Andrew Scott, Manny Jacinto, Tom Blyth, Jamie Campbell Bower, Victoria Pedretti, Jesse Williams, Lucien Laviscount, Thomas Doherty, Katherine Moennig, Costa D’Angelo, Tyriq Withers, Shawn Hatosy, Sam Heughan and Matt Smith.

Stars who have teamed for Quinn duet productions include Love Hypothesis stars Tom Bateman and Lili Reinhart (Olympus U), Heated Rivalry stars Hudson Williams and Connor Storrie (Ember & Ice) and Off Campus stars Mika Abdalla and Stephen Kalyn (Rent Free).

Felled by Emma Parkinson, narrated by Skarsgard, is available now on Quinn.

The Dolphins will reportedly be without one of their top defensive players for the rest of the season.

'Godzilla Minus Zero' Exposes the Biggest Problem with Christopher Nolan's World War II Epic

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Director Takashi Yamazaki made his new Godzilla movie in direct response to Christopher Nolan's film

In 2023, two Oscar-winning blockbuster movies tackled the topic of nuclear weapons and the bombing of Hiroshima and Nagasaki in very different ways. Christopher Nolan's Oppenheimer told the story of the atomic bomb through the eyes of its creator, J. Robert Oppenheimer (Cillian Murphy), while Takashi Yamazaki's Godzilla Minus One continued the 70-year cinematic tradition of using a giant radioactive monster as a metaphor for nuclear warfare.

Notably, neither movie actually depicted those nuclear explosions or their immediate aftermath onscreen, but three years later, one of those directors has done exactly that — and it's not Nolan.

Light spoilers ahead for the plot of Godzilla Minus Zero.

While Nolan followed up Oppenheimer with The Odyssey, which in many ways feels like a spiritual sequel about another brilliant man whose actions changed the course of history (arguably for the worse), Yamazaki delivered a direct sequel in Godzilla Minus Zero. The film, which is currently stomping its way across movie theaters worldwide and dominating the U.S. box office, features a brutal depiction of the immediate aftermath of the bombing of Hiroshima.

Just before the climax of the film, we see a flashback in which one bloodied and stunned character stumbles through the streets of the obliterated city. The camera then points directly at the charred remains of several humans who once lived there. Yamazaki juxtaposes this directly with images of Godzilla's destruction in perhaps the most unsubtle metaphor in a movie released this year.

With that one scene, Yamazaki pulls off the one thing Nolan was never brave enough to do: he shows the actual, awful carnage that fuels both director's Oscar-winning movies. And as it turns out, this isn't a coincidence.

Back in March 2024, right after winning an Oscar for Best Visual Effects, Yamazaki responded to a question comparing his and Nolan's movies with an intriguing promise.

“As a person of Japanese ancestry and descent, my response to Oppenheimer [is that] I would like to dedicate a different film to that when that day comes,” Yamazaki told the press.

Speaking to Time Magazine more recently, Yamazaki offered a bit more insight into how Godzilla Minus Zero became that response.

"In my mind I was thinking I'd develop a film that looks directly at Hiroshima," he said. "But then I began thinking: Wait — Godzilla is itself a metaphor for nuclear war. Maybe I can combine the two and make a Godzilla movie that is also an answer to Oppenheimer."

Godzilla is itself a metaphor for nuclear war.

The result is a stunning cinematic sequence that's in direct conversation with Nolan's film while also pushing the legacy of the Godzilla franchise to a bold new place it's never ventured before. For Yamazaki, however, it's not just about one-upping other movies, it's about showing the horrors of nuclear warfare to a modern audience that might otherwise never see them.

"It was important to show as many people as possible what the effects of an atomic bomb would really look like," he said. "I wanted to evoke something similar because it's very important for it to feel relatable — to jog our imaginations in a way that says, 'Wow. This is real. It is a very violent act.'"

Nolan devoted much of Oppenheimer to exploring the brutal reality of nuclear war, but he never went so far as to actually show us what happened in Japan. The director did offer an explanation for why at the time, though it's not particularly satisfying — especially in the wake of Godzilla Minus Zero.

At a public event in 2024, Nolan explained why he decided not to show the actual bombing of Hiroshima and Nagasaki in his movie.

"The feeling for me as a filmmaker was very strongly that to depart from Oppenheimer’s experience would betray the terms of the storytelling," Nolan said. "He learned about the bombings of Hiroshima and Nagasaki on the radio — the same as the rest of the world."

It's true that Oppenheimer is told from the main character's perspective, but there is one exception. While most of the film is essentially Oppenheimer's POV, several black-and-white sequences intercut throughout show us the same series of events from the perspective of antagonist Lewis Strauss (Robert Downey Jr.), including some events that Oppenheimer never witnessed first-hand.

So it's not like showing Hiroshima or Nagasaki would have broken the rules of Oppenheimer set out by Nolan, it's just that he chose not to do it. Of course, that's a valid choice, even if it's arguably one of the film's only flaws. But with the benefit of hindsight after seeing how Yamazaki depicts that horrifying event — and how just a few seconds of screentime hits harder than Nolan's entire movie — it's hard not to look back at Oppenheimer with a sense of disappointment.

Maybe after watching Godzilla Minus Zero, Nolan himself will feel a similar sense of regret, but considering the director almost never makes sequels, I doubt he'll revisit the events of World War II and the bombing of Hiroshima and Nagasaki any time soon. Thankfully, we have Yamazaki's brilliant Godzilla sequel, which might not even exist if it wasn't for Oppenheimer in the first place.

Colin Farrell's Remake Of A '90s Sci-Fi Movie Is One Of Prime Video's Biggest Streaming Hits

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

"Total Recall" (2012) is directed by Len Wiseman, who most recently oversaw the 2025 "John Wick" offshoot "Ballerina" and previously helmed the "Underworld" films between 2003 and 2016. He is, unfortunately, also responsible for 2007's "Live Free or Die Hard," which, despite making the third spot on /Film's "Die Hard" movie ranking, we simply could have done without.

Unlike the 1990 original, "Total Recall" is set entirely on an Earth ravaged by chemical war, leaving only the United Federation of Britain (UFB) and the Colony (Australia) habitable. Whereas Arnold Schwarzenegger's Douglas Quaid had dreams of Mars, which turn out to be long-buried memories, Colin Farrell's counterpart finds himself experiencing nocturnal visions of being a secret agent. Bored of his factory job on The Colony, he enlists the company Rekall to implant artificial memories to match his dreams, which he soon learns are, in fact, memories of having worked as a UFB agent. Hunted by his former employers, he teams up with Jessica Biel's Melina, a member of a rebel group known as The Resistance. But as the pair try to outrun the UFB and their Chancellor Vilos Cohaagen (Bryan Cranston), the line between fantasy and reality becomes increasingly blurred.

Ahead of the film's debut, /Film asked: "Is 'Total Recall' the least essential film of the summer?" The answer, according to most critics, was yes. But it seems the Prime Video crowds are willing to give it another shot. Per streaming viewership tracker FlixPatrol, "Total Recall" is the No. 3 most-watched movie on Prime Video in the United States as of November 9, 2026. What's more, based on the film's trajectory thus far, it could be set for a chart-topping run. "Total Recall" debuted on the charts at No. 9 on November 7, 2026, before jumping to fourth the following day and hitting No. 3 the day after. By the end of the week, Farrell's forgotten remake might just hit No. 1 — not bad for a film with a 30% critic score on Rotten Tomatoes.

Both Philip K. Dick's story and the 1990 "Total Recall" film feature Mars. But the 2012 remake actually has more in common with Paul Verhoeven's rendition than the novel, using characters that only appear in the movie and adhering more closely to the big-screen storyline. That might have seemed like a reasonable approach, given how the first film was so successful. Unfortunately, Len Wiseman's effort didn't emulate the offbeat comedy of the original, which was so sharply rendered by Arnold Schwarzenegger and gave the film such a unique tone. Despite featuring some impressive action scenes, Wiseman's version also managed to feel like a generic sci-fi actioner, all of which combined to yield a less-than-impressive critical response.

That said, Roger Ebert was impressed enough to bestow three stars on the film — just half a star less than the 1990 version — and called it a "well-crafted, high-energy sci-fi." Still, even he had to admit that the film was one of several sci-fi movie remakes that should have never happened, ending his review by writing, "It never touched me emotionally, though, the way the 1990 film did, and strictly speaking, isn't necessary." Others were much more condemnatory, with David Sexton of the London Evening Standard giving the film one star and writing, "We used to do the future so much better. Let's not forget that. Let's forget this instead."

Normally, that should deter you from joining the "Total Recall" renaissance on Prime Video. But considering that, since the movie's debut, we've been subjected to an onslaught of streaming movie shabbiness, it might be worth a go to see if the remake really is bad or whether that's merely a memory implanted by the critics.
