Roger Ebert Called Disney's Attempt To Update A Classic Cartoon Character 'Transcendently Bad'

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Kids of today likely won't have heard of the visually-impaired cartoon millionaire known as Mr. Magoo. But for a large part of the 20th Century he was a pop culture fixture who entertained generations of kids with his slapstick shenanigans. By 1997, however, his appeal had waned, which makes Disney's decision to reboot the IP with a Leslie Nielsen-led live-action film all the more confounding. Thankfully, Roger Ebert was on-hand to let the studio have it — which he dutifully did with a scathing review that, bizarrely enough, actually awarded the film half a star.

The critic handed out some damning reviews in his time. Ebert hated Leonard Nimoy's box office flop "Funny About Love" and even walked out of the Oscar-winning war movie "Mediterraneo." All of which left filmmakers terrified of being handed the dreaded "thumbs down" for their efforts. Around 60 movies earned this mark of shame during the critic's career but somehow, "Mr. Magoo" managed to eke out half a star. If you read Ebert's thoughts on the film you'll no doubt wonder how it managed such a feat.

In his review, Ebert revealed that the only thing he liked about the film was the titular character's red Studebaker — that was it. Otherwise, he considered "Mr. Magoo" to be so bad it "soars above ordinary badness as the eagle outreaches the fly." How, then, did this ill-advised comedy reboot manage to get a half-star out of the old boy? Did that Studebaker really save "Mr. Magoo" from the thumbs down club? It's hard to imagine given just how much Ebert hated this movie.

There are plenty of movies Roger Ebert hated that are worth watching, but "Mr. Magoo" isn't one of them. The character first appeared in the 1949 theatrical short "The Ragtime Bear," where he was voiced by none other than future "Gilligan's Island" star and legendary character actor Jim Backus. Backus voiced the United Productions of America (UPA) character until 1977, portraying him in theatrical cartoons, TV series, and TV specials. While Mr. Magoo's character developed over the course of his existence, he's best known as a lovably oblivious old millionaire, whose extreme nearsightedness sees him wander into dangerous situations only to miraculously escape unharmed — causing significant distress to his nephew and frequent sidekick Waldo.

While Backus stopped voicing the character after the 1977 TV series "What's New, Mr. Magoo?" the visually-impaired millionaire endured. The most recent incarnation of the character appeared in the "Mr. Magoo" cartoon, which wrapped up in 2023. Prior to that, however, Disney decided that kids of the mid-'90s needed to be reintroduced to Quincy Magoo, and hired Hong Kong filmmaker Stanley Tong to make it happen.

In what turned out to be Tong's one and only English-language film, the frequent Jackie Chan collaborator teamed up not with a martial arts legend but with Leslie Nielsen. The "Naked Gun" star played the eponymous millionaire in a film that saw him hunted by the FBI and criminals eager to recover a priceless gem to which Mr. Magoo is entirely oblivious. Much of the movie follows the tried-and-true formula of its title character getting himself into absurd situations and coming out unscathed, such as when he commandeers an ironing board to ski after thieves. The movie made $28.9 million on a $30 million budget and upset Roger Ebert considerably.

In his half-star review of "Mr. Magoo," Roger Ebert described the film as being "transcendently bad," and accused it of being entirely without laughs. The critic admitted to never having been a fan of the original cartoons, which he recalled as essentially telling the same joke over and over. Likewise, the film was, in Ebert's estimation, "a one-joke movie without the joke."

Interestingly enough, this might be the only time he ever took aim at the bloopers, which play during the film's credits and evidently failed to amuse the critic. "Even the outtakes at the end aren't funny," he wrote, "and I'm not sure I understood one of them, unless it was meant to show stunt people hilariously almost being drowned." Ebert wasn't even impressed with Leslie Nielsen, who'd long since established himself as a comedic force but was, according to the reviewer, "unable to overcome the fact that a little Magoo at six minutes in a cartoon is a far different matter than a lot of Magoo at 90 minutes in a feature."

On "Siskel & Ebert," neither of the hosts could bring themselves to say anything nice about the movie. Gene Siskel called it "uninspired" before Ebert took the opportunity to once again lay into the movie, calling it a "wet blanket." Roger Ebert was wrong about a fair few movies in his time, but a 10% critic score on Rotten Tomatoes suggests he was right on the money with this one. And yet, he gave the movie half a star. Why? Did the Studebaker really save it from the shame of a thumbs down rating?

7 Alan Ritchson Movies & TV Shows Reacher Fans Will Love

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Faith Roswell is a Senior Writer on Screen Rant's Classic TV team. Since earning her degree in Creative Writing over a decade ago, Faith has written articles on film and TV from a variety of different angles. Faith now combines her knowledge of psychology with her love of monster movies to give more insight into what makes the best ones.  You may have read her Screen Rant lists and features covering horror, sci-fi, and fantasy, or read her Amazon Top 10 book, "Movie Monsters of the Deep." Faith has had an extensive career as a writer, appearing on BBC live radio, researching true crime for Rotten Mango podcast, and writing for publications including Mental Floss, Atlas Obscura, and The Daily Jaws before beginning here at Screen Rant.

When Reacher first aired, it appeared to many that Alan Ritchson exploded onto the screen and has barely left since, but while Reacher might be Ritchson's best TV show, his onscreen career began 20 years ago. His early roles made use of his distinctive height and physique, with the younger actor playing Aquaman in the superhero TV series Smallville, Gloss in The Hunger Games franchise, and having his first major role as the linebacker Kevin "Thad" Castle in the TV sitcom Blue Mountain State. However, Reacher then became a gamechanger for the actor.

Reacher instantly drew attention for featuring a more faithful portrayal of Lee Child's iconic character than in Tom Cruise's movies, but the show made some significant changes from the books, allowing Ritchson more versatility. Despite being somewhat typecast, Prime Video's best action series gave him the role that he was born to play, making him into one of the decade's biggest action stars and highlighting his other movies and TV shows both before and since that are must-watches for Reacher fans.

Syfy's gritty, ridiculous sci-fi action TV series is Fallout meets Mad Max, with a side of Death Race. In the dystopian future of an alternate 1999, gasoline and water are ruinously expensive and a mysterious corporation oversees virtually every aspect of daily life. Ritchson plays Los Angeles police officer Arthur "Barbie" Bailey, who is forced to enter the titular "Blood Drive," a race in which the cars run on human blood. The show, which is as gory as its description suggests, was canceled after one season, but it has a surprising 86% positive audience score on Rotten Tomatoes.

A few scenes in season 4 saw Reacher becoming a horror show, but it is firmly grounded in the action genre. That said, the series has become known for its improbable and often hilarious fight scenes, which see Reacher using virtually any object in reach to deal devastating impact. Fans who love this aspect of the series and enjoy the Mad Max franchise should not miss Blood Drive for more of the same, but with even wilder twists.

After playing Aquaman in Smallville and voicing Raphael in the Teenage Mutant Ninja Turtles movie, Ritchson plays his third superhero role in HBO's 49-episode action series, Titans. Ritchson appeared as Hank Hall, aka Hawk, in the first season of the show, with his role being expanded for the second and third seasons. With the show being based on the DC Comics Teen Titans characters, it may not come as a surprise that its target audience skews younger than Reacher's, but the series is well worth watching.

One of Alan Ritchson's most unusual roles has been his work as the body model for Beowulf in the 2007 movie of the same name.

Titans might be aimed at slightly younger viewers but it is by no means a kids' show, and essential viewing for fans of the DC Universe. It is violent, intense, and with Reacher estimated to return in 2027, this leaves more than enough time to start the series. Ritchson gets to play a vigilante with plenty of opportunity to beat up bad guys, but as part of a team rather than carrying the entire series. Titans also stars Titus Welliver (Bosch,) Anna Diop (Us,) and Esai Morales (How to Get Away with Murder.)

The 2026 action comedy movie Runner sees Ritchson starring along Owen Wilson in a race against time, as the two must make an important delivery while being pursued by dangerous enemies. Reacher has cemented Alan Ritchson firmly within a certain type of role, and he plays it very well. In Runner's case, he is a former Special Forces operative, who prefers to keep his own company and has strained relationships with people from his past. Working as a transporter of legal and illegal items, this assignment sees him in charge of a human liver.

With the liver intended for transplant and the cartel in pursuit, the movie is fast-paced, often hilarious, and features a very similar dynamic to the Reacher series, with Ritchson's character acting as the protector and initially reluctant friend to a physically weaker character with skills of his own. Reacher fans will enjoy Runner for its impressive stunts and old-school action tone, and while it was less well-received with Rotten Tomatoes critics, who gave the movie a still-solid 78% positive score, audiences rated it 89%.

Motor City might have received middling Rotten Tomatoes ratings from both critics and audiences on its initial release, but Alan Ritchson's gritty revenge thriller has conquered streaming instead. The movie, which also stars Shailene Woodley (Divergent) and Lionel Boyce (The Bear,) follows John Miller (Ritchson,) who breaks out of prison after being framed, and sets off on a revenge mission against the gangster who framed him and stole his girlfriend.

Aside from being action-heavy, Motor City has a surprising detail in common with Reacher, as both Ritchson's characters rarely speak. This is one of the defining features of Jack Reacher as imagined by Lee Child, but the series made some necessary changes, giving the character more dialogue to replace the books series' interior monologues. People who wanted to see a more accurate version of Reacher in this respect should watch Motor City, which features almost no dialogue at all.

The moments in which Reacher takes down an impressive line-up of opponents single-handedly are some of the best in the series, but the few scenes that show him as part of a group are some of the most heartwarming. With Reacher being a drifter who avoids relationships, the series rarely offers scenes like the ones in season 2 that introduce the dynamic within the 110th Special Investigators, but The Ministry of Ungentlemanly Warfare has plenty. Based on the book of the same name, the movie follows the first-ever Special Forces organization, comprized of a team of misfits using unconventional strategies.

The Ministry of Ungentlemanly Warfare became Netflix's top movie in September, and its stacked cast and genre mix have earned it a 91% positive audience rating on Rotten Tomatoes. Ritchson plays Major Anders Larssen, the glasses-wearing weapons expert whose skill with a bow and arrow reflects the abilities of his real-life inspiration. Like Reacher, Larssen is a man of few words, but Ritchson still trained with a dialect coach to perfect his Danish accent, and described Larssen as "the baddest dude I’ve ever seen on paper in my life" in an interview with The Hollywood Reporter.

The reaction to the announcement of a Neagley-centered spinoff was mixed, with people who loved Maria Sten's portrayal of the iconic character excited to see more of her, while others were concerned that Neagley might not be able to carry an entire show alone. These concerns were quickly laid to rest, with Neagley being a fantastic action series even without Reacher's constant presence, and a perfect replacement for Reacher as we wait for the next season. Unlike most of the movies and TV shows on this list, Alan Ritchson is barely seen, but this was an excellent creative decision.

Neagley was allowed to develop as a character and series without living in Reacher's shadow, so by the time Ritchson reprised his career-defining role, Neagley had already claimed a place among Prime Video's greatest action TV shows. With Neagley renewed for a second season, it remains to be seen how much of a role Ritchson will play, but many fans of both series hope for a few more crossovers, with their dynamic bringing out the best in both characters and actors.

War Machine is as close to a sci-fi Reacher movie as it is possible to get. Instead of insidious government conspiracies and smaller scale villains terrorizing their towns, Ritchson's nameless character is up against a far greater threat. The Reacher star's R-rated thriller sees with a gruelling military training excercise turn into a fight for survival against a machine that starts targeting and killing the team, resulting in reviewers comparing the movie to Predator, Terminator, and War of the Worlds.

Known only as 81, Ritchson plays a Staff Sergeant on a mission to pass training and prove himself as a Ranger, but while he is physically one of the strongest people in the group, he does not bond with them, preferring to work alone until the threat from the machine drastically changes the team's dynamic. The movie received middling Rotten Tomatoes scores, but it is a lot of fun, especially for fans of both Reacher and sci-fi, and a sequel is now reportedly in progress.

Johnny Depp & Ti West Land In A Chilly London For ‘Ebenezer’ Global Premiere

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Christmas came early to London’s Leicester Square on Monday as Johnny Depp touched down for a red-carpet global premiere of Ebenezer in the company of director Ti West and cast including Rupert Grint, Sam Claflin, Tramell Tillman, Ellie Bamber and Robert Strong.

The red carpet was decked out with Christmas trees and artificial snow while 1950s festive classics blasted out in the backdrop, and if by magic, temperatures dropped, ending an unseasonably balmy autumn.

The chilly conditions did not deter fans who crammed into the red-carpet enclosure, most of them there for a glimpse of Depp. He did not disappoint, arriving in a timely fashion to talk to handpicked media and then walk the fan line. Related Stories News Johnny Depp Open To Reprising 'Pirates Of The Caribbean' Role To "Send Them Off On A Better Note" Documentary Joe Perry Bows Out Of Hollywood Vampires Doc Screening As Aerosmith Ramps Up For Hollywood Bowl Gigs

Depp’s latest role as Ebenezer Scrooge is being billed as the actor’s comeback, after being left out in cold following his high-profile legal battles with ex-wife Amber Heard. Watch on Deadline

Rumors are rife that if Ebenezer performs well, it could help green-light a reboot of the Pirates of the Caribbean franchise with Depp reprising his Captain Jack Sparrow role.

Whether this comes to pass remains to be seen but it was plain to see on Monday night that Depp was the main draw, with the public at least appearing to have moved on.

Talking to Deadline on the red carpet, West said he and Depp had bonded over a shared love of Charles Dickens’ ‘A Christmas Carol’ as well as admiration for Alastair Sim’s performance as Ebenezer Scrooge in an 1951 adaptation.

´Ebenezer’ director Ti West talks on casting Johnny Depp as Ebenezer Scrooge: “He [Depp] was obsessed with Scrooge since he was a little kid. When he found out about the movie, he was like, ‘Alistair Sims is a huge reason why I’m even an actor.’” pic.twitter.com/A2zFDV8J5r

“I’ve been obsessed with it since I was little kid. I would read it every Christmas, and I was into it because it was a ghost story and then I saw the George C. Scott film when I was really little and Marley really scared me,” he said referring to Clive Donner’s 1984 version.

“I’ve had a fascination of it and a version in my head that I was waiting for someone to make that they didn’t make. What’s also cool about it is that it’s one of the rare stories that because it’s so enduring, redoing it, is part of the charm,” he said.

West said Depp had approached him about the role when he heard the production was in the works.

“He was obsessed with Scrooge since when he was a little kid also which I didn’t know and so when he found about the movie he was like, ‘Alastair Sim is a huge reason why I am even an actor.’ It was a weird cosmic thing where it was meant to be,” said West.

Depp confirmed West’s account in a public interview ahead of heading inside the cinema.

“I adored it as a child and to this day I know there is no better person to play Ebenezer Scrooge than Alastair Sim… the challenge is to find another angle,” said Depp, praising West for creating a atmosphere on set in which there was space to experiment with the role.

Johnny Depp talks childhood obsession with ‘A Christmas Carol’ and Ebenezer Scrooge at the ‘Ebenezer’ premiere in London pic.twitter.com/hnRAOiODqS

Not surprisingly, given West’s horror filmography, there is a gothic horror to feel to the adaptation, giving the work an extra frisson that goes beyond past big screen versions.

Ellie Bamber, who plays Ebenezer’s long lost love, that it also remains very much a family film. “It’s still very Christmassy with a hint of fright.”

‘Ebenezer’ star Ellie Bamber discusses Ti West’s gothic horror take on Charles Dickens classic: “It’s still very Christmassy with a hint of fright.” pic.twitter.com/EzT3xoTHKa

Johnny Depp Premieres ‘Ebenezer’ in London, Kim Kardashian Wears Gucci to LACMA Art+Film and More Celeb Red Carpet Photos: November 2026

Former porn star Ron Jeremy has nearly a million dollars in assets … despite currently living in a private residence with around-the-clock care due to his advanced dementia ... according to docs filed by his estate lawyer.

2027 Will Give One Piece A New Lead Hero, And It's Not Luffy

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For nearly three decades, Monkey D. Luffy has fronted One Piece, and its theatrical adventures have usually followed the Straw Hat captain or put his crew in a new crisis. That pattern is set to change in 2027. One Piece Film: God Valley explores a chapter of history that unfolded long before Luffy began his voyage.

The title points toward the infamous incident involving Gol D. Roger, Monkey D. Garp, and Rocks D. Xebec. Those three figures appeared in the official announcement teaser, signaling a different generation of heroes and threats. Luffy is not being replaced in the main series; this movie simply has a past-era story to tell.

Toei Animation announced One Piece Film: God Valley on August 23, 2026, during One Piece Day '26, confirming a Japanese theatrical release in summer 2027. The teaser offered no full synopsis, but its focus on Roger, Garp, and Rocks makes the historical setting unmistakable. This is not another adventure built around the Straw Hat crew.

The God Valley Incident took place 38 years before One Piece's present-day events. Luffy's voyage begins in a world shaped by its aftermath, not in the era when these legends were making history. A film set during that conflict naturally shifts attention to the people who fought there, rather than the pirate who inherited their legacy.

Roger is a compelling candidate to anchor the story. He was not yet the Pirate King remembered by the world, and his rivalry with Rocks preceded the final stretch of his journey to the One Piece. The teaser places him alongside Garp, suggesting the movie will explore the unlikely partnership that helped define the incident.

The island gives the story a darker foundation than a conventional treasure hunt. In the manga, God Valley was the site of a World Noble hunting competition, in which the island's people were treated as prey. The arrival of powerful pirates and Marines turned that atrocity into a battle whose truth the World Government later tried to erase.

Garp's role complicates the idea of a single central hero. A Marine, he joined forces with Roger, a pirate, during the crisis. The official version of events elevated Garp as the Hero of the Marines while concealing Roger's part in the battle. A film can dramatize the partnership behind a story the authorities deliberately distorted.

Rocks D. Xebec is not simply a name for Roger and Garp to defeat. He led the Rocks Pirates, a crew that included younger versions of future powerhouses such as Edward Newgate, Charlotte Linlin, and Kaido. The manga's extended flashback also gives Rocks personal history and motivations, making him central to the tragedy rather than a faceless villain.

God Valley appears set apart because its title points to a major event in Eiichiro Oda's manga, rather than another separate Straw Hat adventure. Earlier films have largely told original stories, while this project seems poised to bring a defining piece of the series' history to theaters. That could make its different lead perspective more than a casting change.

The timing adds to the film's importance and excitement. One Piece began serialization on July 22, 1997, so its 30th anniversary falls on July 22, 2027, the same year God Valley is scheduled to arrive. It will also be the franchise's first new theatrical feature since One Piece Film: Red opened in Japan in August 2022.

Weekly One Piece episodes are halting indefinitely, but the anime hiatus clears the runway for a major canon movie.

Toei has not revealed the director, screenplay, voice cast, exact Japanese premiere date, or international release plans. The studio has not formally identified one character as the protagonist, either. Roger may be the clearest candidate for the One Piece film's heroic point of view, but Garp and Rocks are essential to the story.

Paddington: The Musical, which opens in 2027, has set its cast, featuring Arrested Development’s Tony Hale in his Broadway debut.

The musical will be led by Kelvin Dukes (America’s Got Talent) as Tony, Drew Gehling (Waitress) as Mr. Brown, Allison Guinn (Operation Mincemeat) as Lady Sloane and Hale (Veep) as Mr. Curry.

Arti Shah, who originated the role of Paddington on the West End, will reprise on Broadway, with James Hameed also returning as Paddington’s voice and remote puppetry.

The cast of the musical also includes J. Quinton Johnson (Hamilton) as Grant, Leslie Rodriguez Kritzer (The Fantasticks) as Millicent Clyde, Zonya Love (The Color Purple) as Tanya, Jessie Mueller (Waitress) as Mrs. Brown, Serena Parrish (Don’t Say Good Luck) as Judy Brown, Karen Ziemba (Contact) as Mrs. Bird and Chip Zien (Into the Woods original Broadway cast) as Mr. Gruber. Watch on Deadline

Paddington: The Musical will begin previews on Tuesday, March 30, at Broadway’s Al Hirschfeld Theatre ahead of its opening night on Sunday, April 18.

Based on A Bear Called Paddington, written by Michael Bond, and the film Paddington, the musical features music and lyrics by Tom Fletcher, a book by Jessica Swale and direction by Luke Sheppard.

In Paddington: The Musical, a small, lost bear from Peru arrives in London in search of a place to call home. A chance encounter with the Browns leads him into an unexpected world of adventure, where kindness has the power to change lives and strangers can become family. But London isn’t all afternoon tea and friendly faces — and even the seemingly happiest of families can have cracks beneath the surface. So when a mysterious and vengeful villain sets her sights on Paddington, the Browns embark on a thrilling rescue mission, realizing they need this special bear as much as he needs them.

Ariana Grande on First Time Meeting Ben Stiller: “Directing Me in an Orgy Scene in ‘Zoolander 2’”

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Grande and Stiller have since reunited in 'Focker-in-Law,' which releases Nov. 25.

Ariana Grande won’t be forgetting the first time she met Ben Stiller anytime soon.

During a recent episode of The Tonight Show Starring Jimmy Fallon, Grande, Stiller and Robert De Niro made a joint appearance to chat about their new film Focker-in-Law, which releases on Nov. 25.

At one point, host Jimmy Fallon asked the Grammy-winning singer about the first time she met Stiller, to which she replied, “I met Ben in a very different way,” drawing laughter from the audience. Related Stories TV Alex Warren to Host Kids' Choice Awards, Airing on CBS for the First Time; Taylor Swift Leads Nominees General News Pete Davidson Slams Body Shaming of Ex-Fiancée Ariana Grande

“He was directing me in an orgy scene in Zoolander 2 with a hippo and Susan Sarandon,” she revealed.

In the 2016 action-comedy, directed by Stiller, Derek (Stiller) and Hansel (Owen Wilson) are lured into the fashion world again, in Rome, where they find themselves the target of a sinister conspiracy. Grande makes a cameo as part of Hansel’s eccentric romantic circle, which he calls his “orgy” group.

“So right off the bat, we had a comfortability,” Grande added, as Stiller quipped that they had an instant connection due to the unusual encounter. “It sounds like two truths and a lie, but it’s my reality,” the Wicked star said.

The pair have now reunited in Focker-in-Law, which sees Greg (Stiller) and Pam Focker’s (Teri Polo) son Henry (Skyler Gisondo) get wrapped up in family chaos when he decides to marry a strong-willed woman, Olivia Jones (Grande), who appears to be his complete mismatch.

The “Petal” singer also told Fallon that their first scene together in Focker-in-Law was another interesting experience.

“We were thrust into rehearsing the Heimlich scene, where I give you [Stiller] the Heimlich,” Grande said. “So I thought maybe we’ll sit in [director] John [Hamburg’s] office with a script and talk a little, get to know each other a little more and work on the scenes and talk about Greg and Olivia.”

“But no. We met the stunts team, and they were amazing. And they were like, ‘All right, Ben, bend over. Ariana, you’ll get behind and just put your arms around him.’ And I was like, ‘Oh, perfect. Yeah,'” she added with a laugh. “My mom was like, ‘How was your first day, honey?’ I was like, ‘Well, I gave Ben Stiller the Heimlich for a few hours.’ It was crazy.”

Grande’s appearance on The Tonight Show marked her return to the public eye after stepping back from the spotlight once her Eternal Sunshine Tour wrapped in September. The Hollywood Reporter learned in August that the singer would be “taking a step back from visibility” for a “much-deserved break from public-facing work and appearances, which has led to endless, ongoing public scrutiny.” Grande has faced a wave of online commentary surrounding her health and body over the last year.

The 49ers may have stumbled just before their bye. But they rolled to victory after their week off.

San Francisco beat Las Vegas 38-17 on Sunday with some strong defensive play.

The 49ers picked up five takeaways off the Raiders — intercepting Kirk Cousins three times and recovering a pair of fumbles from Brock Bowers and Michael Mayer.

San Francisco dominated the first half with three touchdown drives of at least 12 plays, building a 21-10 advantage at the half.

But Las Vegas’ run game continues to be a non-factor, with Ashton Jeanty tallying just 25 yards on six carries.

The Raiders, who have lost five of their last six, will be back at home to face the Seahawks next Sunday.

10 Most Powerful Characters In Fantasy TV History

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The sky is the limit when it comes to fantasy television. Characters can be as powerful as the story allows, and, most of the time, that allowance is pretty darn extreme. It generally all comes down to the genre-required magic system of the individual series. While any fantasy must have such a system, the way it functions can differ drastically. The power scale in one world doesn't necessarily match that of another. Still, when looking at the fantasy landscape as a whole, there are some clear outliers.

Whether a fantasy TV show falls under the subgenres of high fantasy, urban fantasy, science fiction, or anything in between, there is generally a certain standard of power. Characters won't often be so powerful that they are entirely invulnerable, but it can get pretty close. The circumstances around these individuals typically have to be highly specific, since their very existence will often break the power scale. For the right character, however, it's entirely worth it.

The DC fantasy television series Lucifer ran from 2016 to 2021 and centered on the titular character, Lucifer Morningstar (Tom Ellis), as he abdicates his throne in Hell to live in Los Angeles. Lucifer isn't quite as powerful in the TV adaptation as he is in the original comic series, but he's still something pretty special.

Lucifer has superhuman strength, speed, and invulnerability, but even more jolting is his ability to make humans divulge their truest desires through eye contact alone. By the end of Lucifer, this character basically ascends to true god power. It doesn't get much bigger than that.

Because Supernatural is a monster-of-the-week TV series that managed to keep itself going for 15 excellent seasons, it should be no surprise that the power scale got completely out of control. Various characters and villains became unthinkably strong, but The Darkness, also known as Amara, really took the cake. She is the twin sister of God himself with near-infinite power and abilities. The Darkness can warp reality, snuff out other deities, and resist any spell or weapon.

There are few wizards as iconic as Merlin, and that's especially true in the British medieval fantasy-adventure drama that borrows the character's name. Merlin leaves behind the traditional image of an aged old man, instead diving into the story of a young, inexperienced Merlin of Camelot.

While Merlin doesn't quite have total control of his abilities in this version of the story, there is no question in Merlin that there is true, potentially terrible power here. His magic is raw, unfiltered, and rooted entirely in instinct. Merlin has elemental control and telekinesis, and can even sway the forces of life and death.

Netflix's Stranger Things is another TV series that took its power scale to a whole new level. This is most effectively represented by the character Eleven, who possesses god-level telekinesis that can achieve just about anything she desires. Eleven can throw heavy objects across the room or snap a man's neck with just her mind.

Additionally, Eleven can transfer her consciousness into a "void" that lets her eavesdrop on conversations around the world. Thankfully, Eleven is also full of empathy and shines in circumstances of great love. Otherwise, she would have been a horrifying force.

The long-running British television series Doctor Who is set entirely around the character known as the Doctor, though he (or they, really) comes in many different forms. The Doctor is a humanoid alien who travels across time and space in his sentient time machine called the TARDIS. This naturally gives the Doctor great access to the universe, but it goes far beyond just that.

Regeneration is the name of the game, and the Doctor can take on a brand-new physical form when the previous one is no longer livable. Add in that unyielding intelligence and strategic skills, and there isn't much that could ever stop the Doctor for long.

Prime Video's The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power dives deeper into the story of the Dark Lord Sauron, so we get to see more of his immense power than ever before. In the grand scope, Sauron isn't even the most powerful character in Tolkien's lore. However, in an age when greater villains like Morgoth have been contained, this new Dark Lord was free to execute his own, terrible will.

Identify the cover art while scratching off as little foil as possible.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sauron can shape-shift and is especially skilled at influence, seduction, and manipulation. Though his One Ring will ultimately prove to be his undoing, the addition of this evil tool makes Sauron, for a time, the most truly terrible power of several thousand years.

Game of Thrones gave us a lot of pretty powerful characters, and who could have thought that Bran Stark would end up above them all? The hit high-fantasy series spent a lot of time pushing the Starks to their limits, forcing them to grow in miraculous ways. Arya takes a second-place seat in some ways, thanks to her magical lethality. Still, no one quite compares to Bran, who can see anything and everything as it has and will be.

If that wasn't power enough, becoming King of the recently terrorized Seven Kingdoms sealed the deal. Love it or hate it, Bran became the most powerful character of Game of Thrones.

ABC's fantasy drama series brought many of Disney's greatest fairy tales to the real world, which meant the character Rumpelstiltskin got to stretch his legs. We wouldn't normally consider this classic character to be any more threatening than the Evil Queen or Maleficent. Once Upon a Time put quite a twist on things.

Rumpelstiltskin became a being known as The Dark One. The status granted him a seemingly unlimited reservoir of dark magic, allowing him to become a sort of puppetmaster of the wider world of fantasy. Rumpel was ultimately so overpowered that you never really knew in Once Upon a Time where he might turn up.

The Witcher is another fantasy TV show that is absolutely packed with powerful displays. Geralt of Rivia is quite a force, and we saw Yennefer cause absolute devastation on the battlefield. Still, it's their progeny that takes the prize for being the most powerful. Princess Cirilla has what is called the Elder Blood gene in The Witcher, which basically means he has natural reality-altering power.

While she has to learn to control it, this isn't a form of magic that is learned in the way Yennefer learned her craft. Ciri is a source of power in her own right and can use it to manipulate space and time. Though it hasn't played out on screen yet, she ultimately comes to be known as the Lady of Space and Time.

Loki was never a weakling, but the fact that he was repeatedly defeated and ultimately killed in the Avengers movies demonstrates that he wasn't anything too spectacular compared to other forces. This is no longer true for the particular Loki variant that serves as the protagonist in Disney+'s Loki. As the two-season series progresses, this God of Mischief undergoes a dramatic evolution.

This allows Loki to eventually become the God of Stories, which puts him in charge of sustaining reality. This essentially places Loki at the very top of the power scale, though his being so burdened by this particular purpose makes things a bit more complicated.

5 Best New Netflix Series To Binge-Watch This Week (November 9–13, 2026)

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix’s best new shows to watch this week, November 9–13, 2026, include a gangster masterpiece, a spy thriller, a psychological drama, a royal drama, and a hostage crisis. Netflix continues to deliver some of its greatest new releases of the year during its Fall 2026 slate, which has included massive streaming hits like Below, East of Eden, and The Diplomat season 4. While these are all must-watch picks, the following five series are the newest on the platform and are worth diving into this week.

Last weekend, ScreenRant's top 3 shows to binge-watch on Netflix included Taken: Natalee Holloway, a 3-part true crime docuseries that remains the streamer's #1 most-watched series at the time of writing. Other notable recent additions include The Neighborhood, the fan-favorite CBS sitcom which was just added last week, Will Trent, ABC's hit crime drama series, and Rectify, a critically acclaimed slow-burn psychological drama. Also, make sure to check out SR's fresh streaming guides for Prime Video, Apple TV, Disney+, and Paramount+.

Black Doves is still one of our top new shows to watch on Netflix this week following its season 2 premiere last Thursday, November 5. The Keira Knightley-led spy thriller returned with six new episodes and earned a near-perfect 94% Rotten Tomatoes score, improving on its impressive 92% season 1 RT score. Black Doves currently ranks #5 on Netflix's worldwide viewership chart, following Knightley's secret agent protagonist, Helen Webb, as she spies on her husband, Wallace, the newly appointed British Prime Minister, while also facing threats from within her espionage agency, the Black Doves. For a quick and thrilling binge, look no further than Black Doves season 2.

Under Siege is another great new Netflix series that premiered on Friday, November 6, and is worth watching this week for fans of political thrillers and historical dramas. Under Siege chronicles the 1985 Palace of Justice hostage crisis that took place in Bogotá, Colombia, exactly 41 years before the Netflix series was released. Consisting of 8 episodes, Under Siege follows the real-life experiences of several people directly involved in the revolutionary attack, recreating a woman's journey to find her husband inside the Palace, as well as the nation's military response to the hostage crisis sparked by the urban guerrilla group M-19. With its sharp acting and elaborate story, Under Siege is the best new hidden gem on Netflix this week.

All six seasons and 63 episodes of the hit Starz gangster crime series Power officially arrive on Netflix this Monday, November 9, making it the best new series to binge-watch for a long-term binge on the platform. Created by Courtney A. Kemp (The Good Wife) and executive produced by 50 Cent, who also stars in the series, Power was one of the most popular crime series of the 2010s and is now available to stream on Netflix for the first time ever in the United States. Power follows James "Ghost" St. Patrick, played by Omari Hardwick, a wealthy New York nightclub owner who lives a double life as a dangerous drug kingpin. If you love epic crime thrillers, Power is the best choice for you on Netflix this week.

The Empress is one of Netflix's most highly anticipated new releases this week, with its third and final season arriving on Thursday, November 12. After premiering in 2022, The Empress has become one of Netflix's most celebrated period pieces, blending historical drama with epic romance and political intrigue. The series follows Empress Elisabeth of Austria as she meets and marries Emperor Franz Joseph during the mid-19th century in Vienna. The Empress earned an 89% Rotten Tomatoes audience score and is expected to draw significant viewership this week, especially on global charts. If you've been enjoying The Gilded Age on HBO Max, The Empress is perfect for you this week on Netflix.

The Body is ScreenRant's top recommendation to binge-watch on Netflix this week, with its series premiere set to arrive on Wednesday, November 11. Combining supernatural horror with coming-of-age drama over 8 episodes, The Body follows a rebellious Catholic school dance team who experience prophetic visions after an initiation goes wrong, stirring up a frenzy in their small hometown. Taking place in 2004, The Body features a talented ensemble cast and has a vintage millennial vibe with mind-bending psychological themes. The series was created by Quinn Shephard, best known for Blame and Under the Bridge. If there's only one new show to watch on Netflix this week, it has to be The Body.

Netflix's 40-part legal thriller combines murder mysteries, courtroom strategy, and high-stakes cases across four addictive seasons of legal drama.​​​

A Guide to <i><em>The Gilded Age </em></i>Families: the Scotts, van Rhijns, and Russells

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Making sense of the cousins, fiancées, and adopted sons that keep us fascinated as viewers.

The Gilded Age is ultimately a story about society and class, so it’s unsurprising that family ties and marriages are central to the plot. That also means that it can be difficult to sort out who’s who and how they’re all related. As the fourth season continues to gather steam, here’s a guide to all the players in the Scott, van Rhijn, and Russell families.

Peggy grew up in a prosperous Black family but has a difficult relationship with her father, a pharmacist who is unhappy with her decision to become a writer. Peggy was previously married and had a son, Thomas, whom she believed was stillborn. She later learns that her father lied to her and that Thomas is alive and living with an adoptive family. She meets Marian Brook when she is traveling home to her family and begins living with the van Rhijn family. Peggy becomes a reporter for the prestigious Black newspaper, the Globe, and writes a novel. She becomes engaged to Dr. Kirkland, a physician from a prominent family who treats her when she is seriously ill.

Arthur is the father of Peggy and the husband of Dorothy. He was born into slavery but became the successful owner of a pharmacy in Brooklyn. He has a difficult relationship with his daughter.

Peggy’s mother, Dorothy, is a pianist from Maryland who meets her husband in New York City when he saves her from men who were following her. Peggy is her only child.

When Peggy learns that Thomas, the son she believed had died, is alive and living in Philadelphia with the Spring family that adopted him, he is 3 years old. Shortly before Peggy finds out about him, Thomas dies from scarlet fever along with his adoptive mother.

Dr. Kirkland is a physician whom Peggy’s parents bring to treat her when she has a serious respiratory illness. He knew Peggy’s father when he was a child, and Arthur’s pharmacy career inspired him to become a doctor. He and Peggy date and then become engaged at the end of season 3.

Mrs. Kirkland, Dr. Kirkland’s mother, is from a wealthy Newport family. She expects her son to marry someone from a more pedigreed family than Peggy, and when she meets Mr. and Mrs. Scott, she treats them poorly, emphasizing her family’s status and Arthur’s former enslavement. She doesn’t approve of Peggy’s political activism.

Dr. Kirkland’s father is a pastor in Newport who grew up in a wealthy and well-known family.

Agnes is a widow and mother to Oscar. She was married to Arnold van Rhijn, an investment banker from a wealthy, early New York family. She is an aunt to Marian and lives with her sister Ada in a beautiful home on East 61st Street.

Ada is a devoted sister to Agnes, whom she lives with, and aunt to Marian and Oscar. When she meets Reverend Luke Forte, they marry quickly, and Ada begins living with him in the church rectory, which upsets the balance between her and Agnes. When her husband dies, Ada unexpectedly inherits a fortune, making her now wealthier than her sister.

Marian is the niece of Ada and Agnes, the daughter of their estranged brother. After her father dies, Marian finds out she’s been left no money, and she comes to live with her aunts, from whom she had been estranged. She becomes engaged to the widower Dashiell, who is Agnes’s nephew by marriage and Oscar’s cousin from his father’s side, but she later ends it. She has an on-and-off relationship with Larry Russell, George and Bertha Russell’s son.

An Episcopal reverend, Luke meets Ada after moving to New York from Boston and assuming a position at the Episcopal Church of Saint Thomas. He dies shortly after marrying Ada, leaving her the textile fortune he inherited from his mother.

Aurora, who is a major figure on the society scene, is a cousin of Oscar and the niece of Agnes. She becomes close with Marian through their family connections. Aurora is devastated when her husband, Charles, asks for a divorce.

Oscar is a banker and the calculating young son of Agnes, who is gay and closeted but eager to marry a wealthy woman. He proposes to Gladys Russell, but he loses interest when he believes her family might go broke. Oscar has a secret relationship with John Adams IV, a descendant of the political family. In season 4, he marries Enid, a widow with a fortune who shares his interest in social climbing.

Enid was once a maid for the Russell family, but she was let go after trying to seduce George. Enid reappears in season 2 as the wife of Joshua Winterton, an older man, and inherits his money and his place in society when he dies. When Enid gets to know Oscar, they become close and eventually marry, making her the daughter-in-law of Agnes.

A Cavalier King Charles spaniel, Pumpkin is the constant companion of Ada. Larry Russell rescues Pumpkin from a scary run-in with a horse.

Bertha Russell and her husband, George, move to New York City after his work in the railroad industry makes them very wealthy. Bertha is calculating and desperate to be admitted into high-level Manhattan society. She’s eager for her daughter Gladys to marry someone with a pedigreed background. She is also the mother of Larry.

Bertha’s husband is a cutthroat, often cruel, railroad magnate with a distaste for union workers but is deeply loving toward his children, Gladys and Larry. He moves out of his home after believing that his wife’s interference in her children’s love lives is inappropriate.

Bertha and George’s oldest child is an aspiring architect and Harvard grad with a more modern approach to hierarchy and social norms than his mother. He has a flirtation with Marian Brook.

Gladys is the overprotected daughter of Bertha and George. Her mother is focused on finding the right kind of husband for Gladys. Gladys is proposed to by Oscar van Rhijn, but her father doesn’t allow her to marry him because he questions Oscar’s intent. Gladys wants to be with Billy Cotton, a man she met at a party, but her mother believes she should be with the Duke of Buckingham, Hector Vere, a royal figure. Gladys marries Hector and lives with him in England. They have a child.

Everyone is atwitter when the fancy Duke of Buckingham visits New York. Though he has royal lineage, he has no money. He marries Gladys, knowing that marrying her will bring him significant wealth.

The Marquess of Sidmouth is the son of Gladys and Hector. He is their only child.

Sarah is Hector’s sister and lives with him and Gladys in their castle.

Bertha has a tenuous relationship with her sister Monica, whom we first meet when she attends Gladys’s wedding.

Deadpool 2's Rob Delaney Debunks Popular X-Men Fan Theory About His Character Peter

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

No, X-Men fans, Peter from "Deadpool 2" and "Deadpool & Wolverine" is not Peter Wisdom. Peter actor Rob Delaney confirmed this in a red carpet interview with ScreenRant, and we offer our deepest nerd condolences to anyone who may have been invested in this.

The presence of Delaney's character Peter, a supporting player in David Leitch's 2018 action/comedy "Deadpool 2," is a joke unto itself. Deadpool (Ryan Reynolds) is assembling a team of superheroes called the X-Force that he intends to employ for a skydiving-based rescue mission. Deadpool is such a non-credible deadbeat, though, that he can't afford to be too choosy about who joins his team. He recruits characters like Shatterstar (Lewis Tan), Domino (Zazie Beetz), and Bedlam (Terry Crews), but also deigns to take on Peter, a regular, kind of dorky middle-aged guy with no superpowers.

When the trailer for "Deadpool 2" first dropped, /Film theorized that Delaney could be playing Peter Wisdom, an X-Men character with deep ties to X-Force. The character was first introduced in an issue of the Britain-set X-Men comic "Excalibur," dated February of 1995, and, like all comic book characters, he underwent multiple dramatic life events in a very short amount of time. He dated Shadowcat, became the mentor for X-Force, seemingly died, returned, and became the head of a spy agency. In the comics, Pete Wisdom can absorb heat from the environment around him and channel it into magical "hot knives" that protrude from his fingers.

The character also starred in his own series of comics in 2006 called simply "Wisdom," and he likely has at least a few fans out there. For those who think he appeared in the "Deadpool" movies, though, that wasn't Peter Wisdom. That was just Peter.

The issue of Peter's identity may be a little confusing, as sources like Wikipedia list Rob Delaney as definitely playing Pete Wisdom in both "Deadpool 2" and the 2024 follow-up "Deadpool & Wolverine." On IMDb, however, his proper credit of just "Peter" is retained. In the red carpet interview, Delaney was 100% unambiguous on the matter, saying:

"Peter Wisdom is a guy from the comics. My character is not Peter Wisdom. He's just a guy named Peter. And I think maybe they arbitrarily gave him the last initial 'W,' which explains the confusion. But in fact, [he's] just Peter, created from whole cloth by the writers."

Sometimes actors in Marvel Comics-based movies have to be coy about spoilers and plot details, but it seems that Delaney is not being coy in this case. He seems to genuinely be saying he isn't playing Pete Wisdom.

The arc of Delaney's actual character is pretty hilarious unto itself. Peter is recruited into X-Force, meets a grisly fate, is saved through the miracle of time travel, and then proves to be deeply beloved across the multiverse in "Deadpool & Wolverine." Peter turns up in that film's climax, which involves hundreds of parallel-universe Deadpools pouring through an interdimensional portal to fight each other. When Peter arrives on the scene, however, every Deadpool pauses to marvel at his presence. It seems that all Deadpools have a Peter in their home dimension, and that they all hold him in very high esteem. The hundreds of Deadpools embrace Peter, and he enjoys the attention.

With a universe as long-lived and complex as Marvel Comics, of course, some fans may happily chime in with an optimistic "never say die" attitude about Rob Delaney playing Pete Wisdom. Indeed, as "Deadpool & Wolverine" proved, every character in the Marvel Cinematic Universe has an untold number of parallel doppelgängers from all across the multiverse. Even if the original Pete Wisdom is a suave British James Bond-type with heat knife powers, and the movie Peter is a dippy American everyman, there's no reason why those characters can't be one and the same across the multiverse, right?

And, heck, "Deadpool 2" made a lot of vital changes to known comic book characters, including the aforementioned Shatterstar, a.k.a Rusty. In the comics, Shatterstar is an interdimensional traveler from Mojoworld, something he does explain in "Deadpool 2." But in the movie, he doesn't become a vital member of the team. Indeed, he has an unfortunate run-in with a helicopter. Deadpool, thereafter, quips that no one is going to miss Shatterstar. Nonetheless, the character returned for a very brief cameo in "Deadpool & Wolverine."

So why not do something like that for Pete Wisdom? As of this writing, /Film has been gathering information about the upcoming X-Men film from Marvel Studios, citing a familiar line-up and keeping track of pieces of casting. (Inde Navarrette from "Obsession" has been cast as the character of Rogue, for instance.) Time will tell if the makers of the new X-Men will have enough of a sense of humor to cast Rob Delaney as Pete Wisdom.

Netflix's Upcoming Sci-Fi Spinoff Takes Exciting Step Forward With New Update

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix is preparing for the arrival of one of its highly anticipated spinoffs.

Netflix's upcoming Manifest spinoff series, Arrivals: A New Manifest Mystery, has reached another important milestone as development continues on the science fiction drama. After the original show concluded its run three years ago, the franchise is preparing to introduce an entirely new story within its universe. However, while the plot might be new, the spinoff will maintain its connections to the lore that made its predecessor successful.

Created by Jeff Rake, Manifest originally premiered on NBC in 2018 and followed the passengers of Montego Air Flight 828. Shortly after landing, the characters realized that more than five years had passed despite nothing seeming wrong with their flight. They begin experiencing mysterious events known as Callings, leading them to uncover disturbing secrets about their situation.

Although NBC canceled the series after three seasons, Netflix brought it back for a fourth and final season, which ended in June 2023. The show's popularity eventually led to another installment in the franchise, Arrivals, which Netflix officially announced in August 2026. Now, Rake has revealed that Arrivals: A New Manifest Mystery has secured a director for its first episode. The creator announced the news on his Instagram as part of his Showrunner Diaries series, which provides viewers with behind-the-scenes information.

While Rake confirmed that someone has been selected to direct the debut episode, the director's identity has not been disclosed at the time of this article's publication. The update also revealed that everything is progressing very smoothly across several departments. The creator explained that they are beginning to form Arrivals' production team, with several positions already filled.

Arrivals: A New Manifest Mystery was first announced by Netflix on August 28, 2026, with the date paying homage to Flight 828. The streaming platform confirmed that the upcoming project would feature new characters and storylines while staying true to the Manifest universe.

The spinoff series won't be a direct continuation of the original show's finale. Arrivals: A New Manifest Mystery will explore an entirely different incident within the franchise's world. The story reportedly begins when an unidentified aircraft makes an unexpected crash landing in Denver. The people aboard are unable to remember who they are or why they were on the plane in the first place.

There is no confirmed release date for Netflix's Arrivals: A New Manifest Mystery at the time of this article's publication. However, industry speculation predicts that the series will be released on the streaming platform in 2028.

A supernatural drama series created for NBC, Manifest is an investigation of a fictional flight, Montego Air Flight 828, that went missing for five years after a moment of turbulence in the sky. However, the passengers and crew only felt a moment pass despite the rest of the world assuming they were all gone. The "survivors" now try to reacclimate to society as the world has moved on without them - but voices from beyond seem to beckon them somewhere unknown.

Before Captain America, Chris Evans Starred In An Underrated Non-Marvel Superhero Movie

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Chris Evans is one of the most recognizable faces in superhero cinema, thanks largely to his portrayal of Steve Rogers in the Marvel Cinematic Universe. For nearly a decade, Evans helped bring the First Avenger to life, becoming a central figure in one of the biggest movie franchises of all time. However, his journey into the world of comic book-inspired adventures began long before he picked up Captain America's shield.

In 2005, Evans played Johnny Storm / The Human Torch, in Tim Story's Fantastic Four. He reprised the role in 2007's Fantastic Four: Rise of the Silver Surfer, bringing plenty of charisma and humor to the hot-headed member of Marvel's First Family. Nearly 20 years later, Evans stepped into the shoes of Johnny Storm again with a memorable cameo in Deadpool & Wolverine.

Between his appearances as the Human Torch and his eventual transformation into Captain America, however, Evans headlined another superhero adventure that deserves considerably more attention than it reserves. Released at a time when Hollywood was becoming increasingly fascinated with comic book stories about people with extraordinary abilities, this ambitious science-fiction thriller offered a decidedly different take on the genre.

It featured an intriguing mythology, an international setting, and a collection of characters whose powers made them anything but traditional superheroes. Unfortunately, the film never became the franchise starter its creators might have hoped for.

Push is a 2009 science-fiction action thriller directed by Paul McGuigan and written by David Bourla. Released in theaters on February 6, 2009, the film stars Evans alongside Dakota Fanning, Camilla Belle, and Djimon Hounsou. Rather than adapting an established comic book, Push creates an entirely original world populated by people with extraordinary psychic abilities. Its premise revolves around a secret government organization called Division, which captures and experiments on individuals with special powers in an effort to turn them into living weapons.

Evans plays Nick Gant, a young man with telekinetic abilities who has spent much of his life hiding from Division. His circumstances change when he encounters Cassie Holmes (Fanning), a teenage clairvoyant who can see glimpses of the future. Together, they search for Kira (Belle), a woman with the ability to manipulate people's thoughts and memories, while attempting to stay one step ahead of Division and its dangerous agents. As their mission develops, the characters must use their individual abilities to outsmart an organization that knows almost everything about their capabilities.

What makes Push particularly interesting is how it approaches the superhero genre. Instead of relying on familiar costumes, secret identities, and spectacular displays of power, the movie builds its mythology around different categories of psychic abilities. Telekinetics, clairvoyants, and mind controllers operate within a shared world, and their powers come with limitations that make confrontations more about strategy than brute strength.

The film’s Hong Kong setting also gives it a distinctive visual identity, combining neon-lit streets, cramped apartments, and elaborate action sequences. The result is closer to a supernatural espionage thriller than a conventional superhero blockbuster.

Despite its ambitious concept and talented cast, Push struggled to win over critics when it arrived in theaters. Its complicated mythology and purportedly convoluted storytelling became major points of criticism, with some reviewers arguing that the movie introduced too many ideas without giving them enough room to develop. Rotten Tomatoes currently lists the film with a 23% critics' score, reflecting the largely unfavorable response it received.

Nevertheless, dismissing Push entirely overlooks some of its most appealing qualities. Evans delivers a charismatic leading performance, while Fanning brings an intriguing combination of confidence and vulnerability to Cassie. The film's visual style, creative approach to psychic abilities, and willingness to experiment with familiar superhero conventions give it a personality that separates it from many other genre releases of its era. It also arrived during a particularly interesting period for superhero entertainment, when films like Iron Man and The Dark Knight were helping reshape audience expectations.

Push might not be a masterpiece, but it remains a fascinating chapter in Evans' expansive superhero career. Its original mythology and unconventional approach make it worth revisiting, particularly for viewers interested in seeing the actor outside his familiar Marvel roles. Before he became the face of Captain America, Chris Evans was already leading an ambitious superhero adventure that deserved a little more recognition.

Library by Collective Architecture breathes new life into Scottish high street

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Film and photography by Jim Stephenson

The conversion of a dilapidated high-street building into a public library in Paisley, Scotland, shortlisted for the RIAS Andrew Doolan Best Building in Scotland Award, features in this video by photographer Jim Stephenson.

The film forms part of a series being published by Dezeen in collaboration with the Royal Incorporation of Architects in Scotland (RIAS) to highlight the annual award’s 2026 shortlist.

In the video, viewers are introduced to Paisley Central Library, designed by Scottish studio Collective Architecture to serve as a welcoming community space with reading areas, event spaces and a study room.

According to studio director Ewan Imrie, Paisley Central Library was conceived as a civic space for “people to dwell”.

“The client was very clear from the outset that the building had to be as welcoming to all different types of people from all aspects of society,” Imrie said.

“Encouraging people to dwell was the most important thing and if they pick up a book and read it then that’s a bonus.”

Established in 2002, the Andrew Doolan Best Building in Scotland Award is an annual award that highlights Scottish projects, with this year’s winner set to be announced on 26 November.

Comprising three reuse projects, RIAS said this year’s shortlisted projects tap into “important conversations around community, collaboration, retrofit and reuse”. Shortlisted alongside the Paisley Central Library is the Edinburgh Futures Institute by Bennetts Associates and the Tarlair Outdoor Pool Pavilion by Studio Octopi.

Last year’s winner was a public park renewed by studios Stallan-Brand and LDA Design.

The film and photography is courtesy of Jim Stephenson.

Jaguars' point differential of +108 this season is 50 points better than any other team

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

No NFL team is dominating opponents like the Jacksonville Jaguars.

After blowing out the Ravens 33-10 on Thursday night, the Jaguars have now outscored their opponents by a cumulative score of 229-121 this season. That’s a point differential of +108, by far the best in the NFL.

The team with the second-best point differential in the NFL is the 49ers, at +58. The Jaguars’ point differential is 50 points better than the next-best team.

At 6-2, the Jaguars do not have the best record in the league, as the Bills, 49ers and Vikings are all 6-1. The 49ers are favored to beat the Raiders and improve to 7-1, and the Bills and Vikings play each other on Monday night, so the winner will be 7-1.

But at the midway point of the season, point differential is a better indication of a team’s quality, and a better predictor of a team’s future success, than win-loss record. That’s because with as small a sample size as half a season, a few plays in a few close games can skew the won-loss record, but a few plays don’t skew point differential. Historically, the teams that prove to be the best over the course of a season are the teams that win some blowouts, even if they also lose some close games.

Winning blowouts and losing close games is exactly what the Jaguars do. Both of the Jaguars’ losses were one-possession games that were tied in the fourth quarter, while the Jaguars’ wins have included some massive blowouts: The Jaguars’ 35-point win over the Colts was the biggest blowout of this NFL season, their 29-point win over the Patriots was the fourth-biggest blowout in the NFL this season, their 24-point win over the Browns was the seventh-biggest blowout in the NFL this season, and Thursday night’s 23-point win over the Ravens was the 11th-biggest blowout in the NFL this season.

The Jaguars have won four games by more than 21 points this season; no other team has more than one win by more than 21 points.

Jacksonville has a 3.5-game lead in the AFC South, and six of the Jaguars’ nine remaining games are against opponents with a losing record. They’re poised to run away with their division, perhaps earn the No. 1 seed in the AFC, and maybe blow some teams out in the playoffs just like they have in the regular season.

Joe Milton on his first start: The NFL is about being ready when your number is called

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Joe Milton has never started an NFL game. The closest he came was in Week 18 of the 2024 season when Patriots starter Drake Maye played only three snaps before giving way to Milton, who played the final 61 snaps.

Milton looked like a starting NFL quarterback in that game, completing 22 of 29 passes for 241 yards and a touchdown.

Less than two years later, Milton is on his third team and has played only 51 snaps in four appearances with Dallas since.

He will make his first career start on Sunday when the Eagles play the Giants.

“I was just pretty much going through the plays, going through the week like I was a starter, or like I was playing, and just being ready,” Milton said Thursday, via Olivia Reiner of the Philadelphia Inquirer. “And that’s what the NFL is about: Just being ready when your name is called, and just preparing for those moments.”

The Eagles signed Milton off the Cowboys’ practice squad on Oct. 16 after they placed Jalen Hurts on injured reserve due to blood clots. Tanner McKee started the past three games, but went 0-3 as the Eagles ran their losing streak to six games.

Milton has had a crash course in learning the playbook and the terminology, which he said reminds him of the West Coast scheme he had in New England his rookie season.

Milton was asked how much of the Eagles’ playbook he knows.

“Shoot, whatever’s in this week,” he said with a laugh.

Forget ‘The Traitors,’ Alan Cumming’s 2-Season Apple TV Flop Demands a Weekend Binge

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Alan Cumming is one of the most versatile actors ever. He began as a Tony-winning stage actor and won the hearts of every millennial in films like the Spy Kids trilogy, Romy and Michele's High School Reunion, and Spice World. He then switched to television, where his role as shark lawyer, Eli Gold, on The Good Wife netted him three Emmy nominations. The role that Cumming is perhaps most known for right now is his hosting duties over on The Traitors, a show which has landed him six Emmy wins. Cumming might be crushing his over-the-top Shakespearean-like leadership, complete with a stunning array of colorful costumes, but there's one other binge-worthy project that never got to flourish properly that he shines in.

Schmigadoon! first premiered on Apple TV back in 2021. The parody musical comedy, created by Cinco Paul and Ken Daurio, is an inventive take on the musical art form. The first season, consisting of just six episodes, pays tribute to the Golden Age of musicals of the 1940s and 1950s. With a focus on Brigadoon, and nods to Oklahoma, South Pacific, and Carousel, the show follows two New York-based doctors named Melissa (Cecily Strong) and Josh (Keegan-Michael Key). They decide to go backpacking to try and fix their relationship problems, but on their trip, they discover the mystical town of Schmigadoon. The kooky village seems to exist in some ephemeral past, and there are all kinds of stereotypical, yet hilarious characters who have the tendency to break out into elaborate song and dance musical numbers. Melissa and Josh realize that they can't leave the town until they find true love and break the spell. The second season of Schmigadoon! tackles an entirely different era of musicals. Released in 2023, the six-episode season is aptly named Schmicago, and features parodies of musicals from the 1960s and 1970s, like Chicago, Cabaret, and Sweeney Todd.

Every one of these four started as a cast recording people played until the disc wore out, and every one of them eventually turned into a film. Here is yours, what it says about you, and the case for it being the best of the four.

You work like the clock is against you, because in your head it always is. Hamilton is three hours about a man who cannot stop producing, cannot stop arguing and cannot leave a single thing alone, and you recognised yourself in it immediately.

People made up their minds about you early and they were wrong. Wicked takes the most famous villain in American children's fiction and asks what the story looks like from her side, which is a question you have asked about yourself more than once.

You cannot walk past something unfair. Les Mis is a show about a man who spends his entire life trying to make one wrong right, and about everybody around him deciding what they are prepared to lose for other people.

You are not interested in saving it all for later. Rent is a year in the life of people with no money, no plan and a very clear sense that the time they have is the only thing they genuinely own.

Schmigadoon! is a completely unique show because it's so brilliantly written. There are plenty of fun storylines, and some of the plots that you might already love from these traditional musicals get turned on their heads in a delightfully tongue-in-cheek way. These homages to past memorable musicals are a tad irreverent, but they also focus on the elements that made these shows so popular in the first place. The series is truly at its best during the big-budget musical numbers that are sprinkled throughout each episode. With songs in Season 1 like 'Corn Puddin,' which landed the series an Emmy for Outstanding Original Music and Lyrics, "You Can't Tame Me," "I Always, Always, Never Get My Man," and Kristin Chenoweth's showstopping performance of "Tribulation," Schmigadoon! definitely features catchy, toe-tapping music that will have you wanting to sing along.

It's time to get on your feet and dance!!

Both seasons of Schmigadoon! offer a completely stellar cast of performers. Key and Strong are grounded anchors in this fantastical world, but there are a plethora of other talented actors including Chenoweth, Fred Armisen, Dove Cameron, Aaron Tveit, Ariana DeBose, Jane Krakowski, and Martin Short. Despite this stacked cast, Cumming is a true standout. In Season 1, he portrays the mayor of Schmigadoon, a closeted gay man named Aloysius Menlove who sings the amusing "Somewhere Love Is Waiting For You." Season 2 offers Cumming the chance to chew on even more scenery in an altogether different role as Dooley Blight, a Sweeney Todd rip-off. Through several dark, yet hilarious songs, Cumming manages to make the character even more memorable. He never fails to land each comedic beat, and his beautiful baritone nails every song he takes on.

Although the show was a hit with reviewers, Schmigadoon! was disappointingly cancelled after Season 2 and never seemed to gain the traction with audiences that it needed. In an interesting twist, the show has found new life in a stage musical also written by Paul that debuted on Broadway in April. The show won four Tony Awards this year, proving that the magic of Schmigadoon was transferable from screen to stage. Cumming definitely has his dance card full right now, with his recent role in the miniseries, Tip Toe, as well as his continued participation in the massive hit that is The Traitors, but Schmigadoon! is still certainly worth checking out to get another glimpse of Cumming's immense talents.

Schmigadoon! is available to stream on Apple TV in the U.S.

‘Ghosts’ Fans Waiting for Season 6 Have the Perfect 5-Season Replacement on Prime Video

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Taylor Vollman is a contributor at Collider. She has long quipped, “T.V. is my name, TV is my game.” Fortuitous initials aside, at the heart of a tired joke is a simple truth — she has a lifelong passion for television. And along with it, an affinity for movies, music and pop culture.

In a childhood time capsule, she had listed Friends, PB&J Otter, and Days of Our Lives among her favorite shows. But her tastes have not narrowed with age. Her viewing habits span TV Land classics, network hits, buzzy reality shows and streamer critical darlings.

Hoping to merge this passion with her love of writing, Taylor earned a bachelor’s in media arts and design with a minor in film studies from James Madison University. After working in marketing for years, she wanted to sharpen her skills and went on to graduate with a master’s in journalism from Georgetown University. Taylor has since pursued entertainment writing and reporting.

Rose McIver is no stranger to supernatural comedies. For years before she was communing with ghosts, the New Zealand actress was a crime-solving zombie on the aptly titled iZombie. Premiering in 2015, The CW series ran for five seasons, totaling 71 episodes. It recently found a new home on Prime Video. With its easy access, familiar face, and overlapping themes, it is the perfect show for fans of Ghosts as they bide their time until it returns for its regular season.

The CBS comedy follows Samantha “Sam” Arondekar (McIver) and her husband Jay (Utkarsh Ambudkar) as they transform their recently inherited Woodstone Manor into a bed-and-breakfast in upstate New York. But a head injury leaves Sam able to see and talk with the many ghosts already residing on the property. The network has pushed Ghosts’ upcoming Season 6 to its spring 2027 schedule, except for its holiday specials. The news comes after McIver revealed she will be absent from the start of the season, having recently welcomed her second child. But have no fear, the ghosts still have someone to talk to since Ben Feldman’s Kyle was promoted to a series regular. Ghosts recently aired its Halloween special on October 29, finally revealing Pete Marino’s (Richie Moriarty) fate after his Season 5 cliffhanger. It will also return with a two-episode “Ghostmas” special on December 10, 2026.

iZombie follows McIver as Olivia “Liv” Moore, a Seattle-based medical resident who becomes a zombie after an unfortunate boat party and subsequently upends her life. Keeping her newfound appetite a secret from nearly everyone, including her former fiancé Major Lilywhite (Robert Buckley), she goes to work in a morgue for unlimited access to brains. But a diet of brains comes with a side effect — Liv takes on the personality of the deceased and sees flashbacks to their life. Putting this newfound ability to good use, she pretends to be a psychic and partners with Detective Clive Babineaux (Malcolm Goodwin) to solve murders.

She soon finds a confidant in her boss Ravi Chakrabarti (Rahul Kohli), who makes it his mission to find a cure. Liv isn’t the only zombie in town, with the outbreak’s origin an early mystery within the series. David Anders’ Blaine DeBeers and Bryce Hodgson’s Donald "Don E." Eberhard run a brain-dealing syndicate to meet the dietary demands of its new population. The series manages to balance multiple genres — part comedy, part procedural, and part horror.

iZombie is based on the comic book series of the same name, created by Mike Allred and Chris Roberson. It was published by DC Comics’ Vertigo imprint. The CW tapped Diane Ruggiero-Wright and Veronica Mars creator Rob Thomas to adapt the story for the small screen. The series also stars Aly Michalka as Liv’s best friend Peyton Charles.

Woodstone is getting a reality TV takeover.

While a ghost-infested bed-and-breakfast and a zombie-infested Seattle don’t seem similar on paper, the series share more than just a leading lady. Ghosts and iZombie have a similar comedic sensibility, viewing subjects often seen in horror through an approachable lens and using humor to undercut their more grotesque elements. Ghosts uses crude phrases when approaching bigger afterlife themes, like getting “sucked off” to heaven or Alberta Haynes (Danielle Pinnock) declaring the evil Elias Woodstone (Matt Walsh) “went down on us” when dragged to hell. Similarly, iZombie leans into its comic book origins with its character and prop design. Liv just has a jagged haircut and pale complexion rather than a deformed face and slow-moving gait. The brains she eats even have a cartoonish quality, with the series interspersing Food Network-style cooking montages as Liv tries to add some variety to her all-brain diet.

McIver has made a career of hiding her supernatural abilities, often resulting in bumbling excuses and cringey results. While she isn’t solving actual crimes in Ghosts, she is often getting to the bottom of the ghosts’ many questions and familial mysteries. In either circumstance, Liv and Sam have unexplainable knowledge and the fibs they use as cover typically cause more hijinks. Plus, Liv has the added issue of a new personality every week, sometimes at odds with her petite appearance. One week she could be a Martini-drinking snob, the next embodying a frat boy, or even bursting into song while seeing through the eyes of an actress. Luckily, both characters have at least one confidant, iZombie’s Ravi and Ghosts’ Jay, who help cover her tracks even if they are then dragged along with her.

But more than comedy, both Ghosts and iZombie explore themes of reinvention, mainly through McIver’s characters. In Ghosts, she leaves behind most of her journalistic dreams to open a bed-and-breakfast. And with her new powers, she also prioritizes the happiness of her dead roommates. This new move pushes her to pursue other writing ventures she can accomplish at Woodstone Manor, like writing a book or screenplay. And while their aspirations are not the same, Liv leaves behind her picture-perfect dream of being a doctor and marrying Major. She instead finds new meaning in not only solving crimes but eventually helping other zombies.

Aside from its upcoming Christmas special, Ghosts fans have at least a couple of months until the CBS comedy returns with its regularly scheduled season. So, while they try to fill their time, iZombie is the perfect replacement with adjacent subject matter, familiar comedy, and manageable length. Plus, fans will also get to see the full extent of McIver’s acting chops on iZombie, as she essentially reinvents her character each week.
