Jake Gyllenhaal’s 124-Minute Disaster Thriller Officially Makes a Streaming Comeback

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Erielle Sudario is a Collider News and Feature Author from Australia and has worked in the journalism industry since 2018. She has a passion for entertainment and pop culture news and has interviewed YouTubers, voice actors, film directors, and musicians throughout her career. When she's not writing, you can see Erielle building keyboards, reading a good book, playing video games, and creating content on social media.

One of the biggest sci-fi disaster films remains a streaming hit. Starring Jake Gyllenhaal and Dennis Quaid, the film was one of the highest-grossing movies of its time and explored one of the biggest issues facing our planet. This issue remains relevant today, so it's no surprise audiences still tune in to watch the film.

The film in question is The Day After Tomorrow, a natural disaster movie directed by Roland Emmerich, who has also worked on other notable apocalyptic sci-fi projects. Gyllenhaal plays Sam Hall, who becomes stranded in New York City with his classmates after a massive storm caused by climate change triggers a new ice age. Since its release, the film has grossed over $552.6 million worldwide, making it the sixth-highest-grossing film of 2004.

Recently, The Day After Tomorrow became available on Netflix, and nearly a week after its arrival, it continues to attract viewers. The film still sits at No. 1 on Netflix's Top 10 Movies chart in the United States, with Kidnapped in Her Own Home: The Martha Carelli Story sitting below it. Despite its continued success on the streaming charts, The Day After Tomorrow received mixed-to-negative reviews, earning a 44% critics' score and a 50% audience score on Rotten Tomatoes.

If you enjoyed The Day After Tomorrow, several other movies share a similar premise. One of the easiest recommendations is to check out some of Emmerich's other projects, such as Independence Day, Godzilla, and White House Down, but the one you should definitely stream is 2012. This 2009 apocalyptic film was inspired by the 2012 phenomenon, which involved predictions that the world would end that year. The film grossed $791.2 million worldwide, making it the fifth-highest-grossing film of 2009. It stars John Cusack (Sixteen Candles), Chiwetel Ejiofor (Backrooms), Amanda Peet (The Good Wife), Oliver Platt (The Bear), and Tom McCarthy (13 Reasons Why).

Another movie to consider watching is San Andreas, a disaster film starring Dwayne Johnson. Directed by Brad Peyton, who also worked on Rampage, the film centers on a massive earthquake along the San Andreas Fault that devastates the U.S. West Coast. San Andreas also stars Alexandra Daddario (2010's Percy Jackson & the Olympians: The Lightning Thief), Carla Gugino (2026's Carrie), and Ioan Gruffudd (2005's Fantastic Four) and grossed over $475 million worldwide.

The Day After Tomorrow is available to stream on Netflix. Follow Collider for more updates.

The Day After Tomorrow examines the consequences of abrupt climate change as a series of extreme weather events ravage the planet. A climatologist must navigate the frozen landscape to save his son in New York, highlighting the urgency of addressing environmental challenges and the resilience of human bonds amidst adversity.

Foot Fetish Encounter Leaves a Body in the Freezer in Singapore Dark Comedy Taiwan Creative Content Fest Project ‘Build to Order’

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

A young Singaporean couple who sell pictures of the wife’s feet online end up with a corpse in their freezer in “Build to Order,” the debut feature from director Huang Junxiang, which is at the Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF) in Taipei this week.

Singaporean couple Penny and Allan both work in real estate. They wed young so they could get a public housing flat, but property prices that keep climbing still leave them strapped. To bring in extra money, they put photos of Penny’s feet up for sale online. Then a rich client with a foot fetish offers them a staggering amount for the chance to suck her toes face to face, and they say yes. Related Stories 'Beast in Me' Season 2 Casts John Goodman 'Beef' Creator Lee Sung Jin Sets Netflix Projects With Ali Wong, Gene Hong Under New Banner Lila Pictures

The meeting goes badly wrong. The man suffers an allergic reaction, Allan’s amateur attempt at first aid fails and the client dies. In a panic, the couple stash him in their freezer. Months on, his widow, who has no idea what happened, taps them to list her luxury mansion – drawing the pair ever deeper into greed and guilt as they scramble to save themselves.

“When I was looking to buy my first home in Singapore, every property agent I met warned me that making the wrong decision would cost me financially in the long haul,” Huang says. “They said that I can’t make an emotional decision, I needed to make a logical one. In an expensive society like Singapore, I feel that many decisions are made rationally, at the cost of our identities and our relationships.”

The film is now in post-production, and Huang says finishing it has his full attention.

“I hope our audiences will be entertained by this dark comedy with genre moments and think a little more about the choices they make and the lives they want to lead,” he says.

Producer Macarius Chia says the film deals with a subject that resonates around the world – housing prices and what it costs to get by. “We were also entertained by the humorous script which takes the story in a few surprising directions, without losing sight of the character’s journey.”

Executive producer Lim Teck says he believes in Huang’s vision and that the film could become a breakout hit out of Singapore, “where housing and climbing the social ladder are always such hot topics.” Lim adds that the film is reasonably budgeted and has commercial potential.

In Taipei, the team wants to raise gap financing to finish post, along with partners for audio post and color grading. Chia says they also hope to find “a like-minded international sales agent who believes in the project as the story is accessible.”

For Lim, TCCF is a chance to introduce the project to friends across Asia’s film industry. “We believe our film has a chance to travel and do well in various Asian markets,” he says.

The Taiwan Creative Content Fest, organized by the Taiwan Creative Content Agency (TAICCA), runs Nov. 6–9 at the Taipei Nangang Exhibition Center.

Netflix's 8-Part 'Squid Game Meets Stephen King' Series Is Officially Taking Over The World & Season 2 Is Possible

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix's Squid Game has absolutely nothing to do with Stephen King's horror. One might find a few thematic overlaps between King's stories and Squid Game, but the hit Netflix K-drama lacks traditional horror elements. Interestingly, though, a new show on Netflix seems to perfectly combine Squid Game survival horror with Stephen King supernatural terror.

The show in question, Dead-End Job, follows a simple premise. It focuses on a broken and desperate man, Hyuk‑jun, who decides to take up part-time jobs through a mysterious agency. Soon, every job turns into a battle for survival, reminding one of Squid Game's titular games. With every job comes a supernatural threat, seemingly straight out of a Stephen King book.

Dead-End Job's Squid Game parallels become even more evident when it gamifies Hyuk‑jun's jobs. With every job, Hyuk‑jun has a ticking clock over his head, and he is expected to navigate a set of rules and conditions to be able to survive. Like Stephen King's books, the Netflix horror series also finds terrors in the mundane and everyday struggles of blue-collared jobs.

In its 8-episode runtime, Dead-End Job even features Stephen King-esque monsters like an evil clown, a deadly mist, and a self-driving car. Considering how brilliantly Dead-End Job gamifies its horror by combining elements from both Squid Game and Stephen King's stories, it is not surprising that it is performing incredibly well on Netflix.

In the last week of October, Dead-End Job rose in the ranks to become one of the most-watched non-Engish shows on Netflix for the duration (via Tudum). It has already garnered over 5 million views just a few days after its release. Considering how more and more viewers are checking it out, its viewership numbers will likely increase in the second week.

Dead-End Job's early success makes sense. It is not exactly a Squid Game replacement. However, it scratches the same survival horror itch with its gamified format and supernatural twists. It also keeps viewers glued to their screens by introducing a new part-time job/game in every episode while delivering subtle social commentary on real world concerns surrounding capitalism and job insecurity.

It is almost surprising that Dead-End Job is currently being touted as a limited series on Netflix. Its storytelling format holds immense potential, and the fact that it adopts an original story leaves a lot of room for more follow-ups. Not to mention, Dead-End Job also ends with a massive cliffhanger that perfectly sets the stage for another season.

In the past, Netflix and other streaming services have not shied away from renewing miniseries for more seasons. Given how well Dead-End Job is performing on Netflix, it also seems to have a good chance of returning in the near future. However, the Netflix horror show's return will primarily depend on how it performs in the upcoming weeks.

If Dead-End Job's viewership only grows from strength to strength moving forward, it could become Netflix's next Squid Game. Even if it does not match Squid Game's virality, it has the potential to etch its own solid identity in the horror genre and potentially beat some of Stephen King's direct TV adaptations. Hopefully, Dead-End Job will get to return for at least one more season.

‘Spider-Man: Brand New Day’ Propels Sony Pictures To Huge Q2 Profit Boost

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The friendly neighborhood Spider-Man has swung Sony Pictures Entertainment (SPE) to a massive hike in second quarter profit.

The box office success of Spider-Man: Brand New Day was the main reason SPE posted earnings of $488 million for the three months to September 30, a huge 527% swing on the $93 million posted in the period last year on a dollar basis.

Adjusted operating income before depreciation and amortization was also up to $670.4 million from $183 million in 2025. Revenues came in at $3.2 billion, up 37% on last year, with theatrical sales growing significantly, largely thanks to SPE’s web-handed super hero. Spider-Man: Brand New Day released at the end of July and posted historic North American box office numbers, surpassing Avengers: Endgame‘s three-day record. That set in motion a tidal wave of huge numbers that have propelled SPE to its huge Q2 figures today, and it is now the third-highest grossing movie in history. Watch on Deadline

In August, the movie became the highest-grossing Hollywood movie of all time in India, as it became the second-fastest film in pass $1 billion at the international box office, trailing only Avengers: Endgame. It was already the second-fastest to hit $1 billion at the global box office, and set a bunch of other records.

This time last year, SPE generated $2.3 billion in revenues on $93 million in profit, which was a 25% dip on the previous year.

Overall, media and electronics giant Sony posted Q2 2026 group revenues of $3.43 trillion ($21.5 billion), up 10%, with operating income coming in at 622.3 billion yen ($3.9 billion), which is an increase of 45%. The yen to dollar exchange rate conversion was 159 yen to $1.

Sony will be anticipating more financial gains in coming weeks with Grand Theft Auto 6 set to release on Sony PlayStation 5 and the Xbox. The game is among the most-eagerly awaited in history, with fans holding on for a full 13 years from the previous instalment.

FX's 27-Episode Sci-Fi Masterpiece Deserves Kudos For Breaking Every Storytelling Rule

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For all that sci-fi television has become much more experimental narrative formulas in the age of streaming, one corner of the genre remains surprisingly attached to tradition: superhero shows. Even the most adventurous and ambitious superhero TV shows seem to eventually fall back into old storytelling habits. Even the series that deliberately lean into strangeness for a selling point do so with the promise that everything will eventually click into place and the true heroes and villains of the story will meet for a final showdown no matter how morally ambiguous they’d been up until that point.

That makes an often overlooked 2017-2019 FX series is deserving of much more recognition. Before Disney+ explored superhero surrealism through MCU shows like WandaVision and VisionQuest and Prime Video lambasted the entire genre with The Boys and Invincible, Legion was already dismantling expectations. Creator Noah Hawley was tasked with making a Marvel adaptation, and instead delivered a series that treats every scene as a chance to experiment. The show’s 27 episodes are dripping with confusion, discomfort, and unpredictability, and it makes for some thoroughly entertaining viewing.

In terms of the show’s premise, Legion follows David Haller (Dan Stevens), a psychiatric patient who discovers his mental health struggles are linked to extraordinary mutant abilities. While the series is loosely connected to Fox’s X-Men movies and draws from Marvel Comics mythology, Legion’s plot stands on its own, and Noah Hawley used that independence to turn what could have been a standard superhero TV show into something truly unique.

From the moment it begins, literally nothing about Legion plays into expectations surrounding its plot and narrative structure. For example, most superhero shows spend time establishing a status quo for reality before introducing something that threatens it. Legion makes reality itself the problem. Memories can be manipulated, seemingly ordinary spaces become mental prisons, and it’s always unclear whether a scene is actually happening or just playing out inside someone’s head. Because David’s mental state means he can’t trust his own experience, the same applies for viewers too.

This combination of atmospheric surrealism and an incredibly unreliable narrator defines how Legion’s story is told. The show’s 27 episodes include some incredibly avant-garde installments. Season 1's “Chapter 7” turns escape from a mental institution into a black-and-white silent film sequence, while “Chapter 10” in season 2 depicts the confrontation between David and central antagonist The Shadow King using dance. This psychedelic approach gives Legion’s psychic conflict its own unique style, making the show’s superpowered struggles more unsettling than a simple exchange of energy blasts or city-leveling fist fight.

It’s not only the scene-by-scene storytelling in Legion that flips expectations on its head. The wider narrative also dismantles a core theme of almost all superhero shows: discovering immense power means inheriting a heroic destiny. David eventually comes to see himself as burdened with glorious purpose, but his self-perception as a savior and the reality of his place in the world couldn’t be more different. His certainty that he deserves love and redemption increasingly justifies the harm he causes others, inverting the traditional message that power, heroism, and responsibility all go hand-in-hand.

Several superhero shows that arrived after Legion have also embraced unreliable perspectives, unconventional formats, and morally compromised "heroes". Few, however, are able to sustain the sense that even their storytelling methods can unexpectedly change at any moment across three whole seasons. Legion may not be the most accessible series out there, but its experimental style shapes the entire experience. Its underlying uncertainty is inseparable from its explorations of what ideas like “truth” and “reality” even mean when everyone’s perception is subjective, and this thematic interest in turn means it breaks almost every narrative rule.

Calling Legion a superhero show is accurate as a description of its source material. The show incorporates X-Men characters such as David himself, the Shadow King, and even Professor Charles Xavier. As a description of actually watching it, however, the “superhero TV show” label is incredibly misleading. Sure, there are genre staples like mutants, secretive organizations, and potentially catastrophic powers, but the show itself has little interest in using these elements to construct a good vs. evil superhero narrative about saving the day.

If one were to assess Legion in a vacuum without considering its X-Men roots, psychological horror is a much more appropriate genre label. David’s mind is a thoroughly unsafe place where childhood memories conceal terrifying intrusions from outsiders, and apparent understanding is often just a trick to disguise more secrets. Alongside psychological horror, Legion also works as an experimental sci-fi series, using concepts like psychic spaces and, eventually, time travel to explore identity, responsibility, and the desire to rewrite painful experiences.

There’s also romance to be found, but once again, this isn’t the kind of love story usually found in superhero shows. David’s relationship with Syd Barrett (Rachel Keller) initially seems like it will be the source of a glimmer of hope, that it’s going to be a tale of two isolated people finally finding connection. However, like everything else in Legion, the love story soon gives way to some unsettling explorations of themes like consent, bodily autonomy, and manipulation. Since David and other characters can violate boundaries in ways ordinary people physically cannot, this leads to some truly disturbing moments.

That makes Legion the perfect superhero TV show for anyone suffering from superhero fatigue. It’s not a superhero story: it’s a deep and complex drama that just so happens to center on characters who first appeared in comic books. Its three-season arc is a psychedelic tragedy that uses its character’s spectacular abilities as tools to explore some truly dark ideas. Legion may technically be a superhero series, but it’s one that feels ideal for viewers who openly balk at them.

‘Sounds of the Desert’ Explores Lives, Music and Cosmic Visions of Two Members of the Sun Ra Arkestra (Exclusive Tallinn Trailer)

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

“In a time of displacement and division, their music reminds me that we can still build a shelter together,” says Lithuanian filmmaker Emilija Škarnulytė, whose new doc premieres at Estonia’s Black Nights Film Festival.

The lives, the music and the cosmic visions of two members of the Sun Ra Arkestra, one of jazz’s longest-running avant-garde ensembles, get a spotlight in a new documentary.

Sounds of the Desert, the new film from Lithuanian writer, director, producer and artist Emilija Škarnulytė (Burial), follows Abshalom Ben Shlomo, who died in 2024, and Marshall Allen, who is still performing at age 102. The cinematic meditation on the avant-garde musicians unfolds through interviews, archival footage and live performance. To the sounds of the Sun Ra Arkestra’s music, the film travels through landscapes shaped by and tied to its sound, such as the Arkestral House of Sun Ra in Philadelphia, Chicago, a forest in Lithuania and the Negev Desert.Set to world premiere on Nov. 18 in the Rebels With a Cause Competition at the 30th edition of the Tallinn Black Nights Film Festival (PÖFF) in Estonia, Sounds of the Desert dives into the Arkestra, which was founded by Sun Ra in Chicago in the mid-1950s and brought together avant-garde music, cosmic philosophy, ritual and spectacle. The ensemble became a foundational influence on Afrofuturism and imagining outer space as a realm of Black freedom. Related Stories Movies Milcho Manchevski's 'Sister Brother Manhole Cover' Wins Top Prize at Tokyo Film Fest Movies AFM: Dark Sky Takes 'American Dollhouse' for U.S. (Exclusive)

Škarnulytė wrote, directed, produced and executive produced the film via her company Mirror Matter Productions. The development producer behind the doc is Norway’s Mer Film, which has also been on board of such recent festival hits as Harry Lighton’s Pillion (2026), Joachim Trier’s Sentimental Value (2025) and Magnus von Horn’s The Girl With the Needle (2025).“I learned that home is not always somewhere we return to; sometimes it is something we create from what we carry,” Škarnulytė says. “My decade-long friendship with Abshalom Ben Shlomo opened a path into the Arkestra’s universe, where music holds memory, and imagination makes other ways of living possible.”

And she shares: “I wanted Sounds of the Desert to move through that universe, listening to the echoes between people, landscapes and histories. The Arkestra created a cosmos not to turn away from the world, but to find the strength to live within it and transform it.”

Concludes the filmmaker: “In a time of displacement and division, their music reminds me that we can still build a shelter together, one that travels with us wherever we go.”THR can now exclusively premiere the trailer for Sounds of the Desert. You really don’t need a longer written set-up to dive into this teaser for the musical and visual journey that awaits you.

‘Spider-Man: Brand New Day’ Spins Up Massive Numbers for Sony’s Quarterly Earnings

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Blockbuster “Spider-Man: Brand New Day” delivered a major boost to Sony‘s September quarterly earnings on the top and bottom lines.

Sony Pictures Entertainment, for the quarter ended Sept. 30, reported sales up 37% to $3.23 billion and operating income of $488 million — a year-over-year increase of 527%. The results were largely driven by the box-office success of “Brand New Day.”

SPE’s theatrical revenue for the period came in at a whopping $1.28 billion, an 11-fold increase from the year-earlier period, thanks mostly to “Spider-Man: Brand New Day” alongside horror films “Insidious: Out of the Further” and “Resident Evil.” Television productions segment revenue dropped 31% year over year, to $483 million. Related Stories Tubi Partners With TikTok to Create 10 Original Long-Form Series (EXCLUSIVE)

“Spider-Man: Brand New Day,” which opened wide July 31, became the third-highest grossing movie in history with a haul of $2.52 billion at the global box office this summer (behind the original “Avatar” and Marvel’s “Avengers: Endgame”).

Overall for the quarter ended Sept. 30, Sony’s Q2 of fiscal year 2026, the company reported revenue of 3.429 trillion yen ($21.5 billion), up 10%, and net income of 450 billion yen ($2.8 billion), up 44%.

The Japanese electronics and media conglomerate also saw robust gains in its games division. Revenue for the September quarter rose 9%, to 1.208 trillion yen ($7.6 billion), on higher first- and third-party game title sales partially offset by a drop in PlayStation hardware sales. Sony reported 2.6 million PS5 unit sales in the period vs. 4.0 million in the year-prior quarter. PlayStation monthly active users at the end of the quarter were 127 million, up 2 million sequentially.

“Spider-Man: Brand New Day,” starring Tom Holland as Peter Parker and Zendaya as MJ, is “not so much a superhero film, but ‘super-person’ film,” Tom Rothman, chairman and CEO of Sony Pictures Entertainment’s Motion Picture Group, said during a convo with Variety‘s Cynthia Littleton at France’s Lumière Summit in September. “It’s very human. It’s very emotional. It’s got plenty of whiz-bang, but that’s not what the secret is. The secret is it’s a relatable story that almost everybody has related to.”

The conversion rate Sony used in reporting earnings was $1 to 159.3 yen.

Diana Ross Sets One-Off Australia Concert

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Queen of Motown will headline the Sydney Summertime concert series next March for an exclusive, one-night-only concert.

Diana Ross will head to Australia next year for her first concert in these parts in more than two decades.

The Queen of Motown will headline the Sydney Summertime concert series on Friday, March 5, for an exclusive, one-night-only concert, presented on the eve of the Sydney Gay and Lesbian Mardi Gras Parade.

The superstar singer will bring Diana Ross Freedom in Motion to Centennial Park for the one-off spot, reads a statement from Black Peach Touring, the concerts promoter launched last year by Jessica Ducrou, co-founder and former CEO of Secret Sounds Group. Related The Lettermen's Tony Butala Dies at 87 Karol G Sweeps Latin Airplay's Top 3 With 'No Me Arrepiento de Sentir Tanto' Tracks Steve Ellis, Love Affair Singer, Dies at 76 Explore Diana Ross See latest videos, charts and news

“Diana Ross is an absolute icon and to have her choose Sydney Summertime for her only Australian show, one night only in Centennial Park, on the eve of Mardi Gras is exactly the kind of unforgettable moment we created this series for,” comment Ducrou and Elise Huntley, co-founders and directors of Black Peach, in a statement. “It’s going to be something very special.”

Ross is the third international headliner confirmed for Sydney Summertime’s inaugural 2027 season, following The Strokes and Kings of Leon. Joining the legendary U.S. artist on stage will be British electronic duo Groove Armada for a DJ set, with additional performers to be announced shortly.

The all-ages concert series is supported by Destination NSW, the lead agency for the visitor economy in the NSW government.

Ross, who boasts a string of hits as leader of the Supremes and as a solo artist, is “one of the most iconic performers in music history, with a career spanning decades and a catalogue of hits loved by generations of fans,” comments New South Wales minister for jobs and tourism Steve Kamper.

“To have an international superstar of her caliber headline at Sydney Summertime for an Australian-exclusive performance is a major coup for NSW,” adds Kamper. “Events like this are a powerful driver of tourism. They bring more visitors to our state, generating economic benefits for local businesses and jobs, and reinforce NSW’s reputation as a destination for world-class live music and entertainment.”

The dynamic diva last performed in Australia back in November 2006 for concerts in Melbourne (Regent Theatre and Flemington Racecourse) and Sydney (Capitol Theatre).

Presales for her Sydney Summertime concert open at 9am local time next Monday, Nov. 9. Visit blackpeach.co/dianaross for more.

What Election Day Is Really Like for NBC News' Steve Kornacki

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

NBC News' Steve Kornacki did not wear his signature khakis to cover the United States’ 2026 midterm elections on Nov. 3. But here's what his typical election night routine looks like.

Steve Kornacki has election night all mapped out.

The NBC News chief data analyst returned to cover the 2026 midterm elections on Nov. 3, appearing on the network's 13-hour marathon in what he described as “livestream history.”

“We’ll go nonstop through the night,” Kornacki wrote on X. “Truly hope you’ll come along for the ride.”

While the 47-year-old opted not to wear his signature khakis for this year's shift—opting for a pair of dark trousers instead—he likely will need plenty of caffeine to sustain him during his all-nighter with the “Big Board.”

“Lots of Diet Coke,” he told GQ in 2020. “I'll drink a ton of Diet Coke. I think that's the main thing. Once we start getting election results, I feed off the energy. There's constant stimulus.”

Despite being a self-proclaimed numbers guy, Kornacki often loses count of how many Diet Cokes he drinks throughout his newscasts. As he put it, “It’s just sort of a constant supply.”

“On a normal day, I'll have a couple,” Kornacki explained. “But on election night, I just keep it nearby and I’m just kind of regularly using it.”

So much so, the soda has become his sole source of sustenance during his around-the-clock coverage.

“I do feel eating slows me down,” he shared. “It will make me sleepy. That’s the thing I have found, is that the fatigue will catch up to me when I start eating.”

That’s why also why the political correspondent doesn’t eat at all on election night.

“I'll try to have something during the day. Maybe it's a bagel or something,” Kornacki continued. “I haven't had breakfast in 28 or 29 years.”

In fact, Kornacki only added another beverage to his rider for this year's shift: Dunkin' coffee. Still, the journalist affectionately known to fans as "Map Daddy" doesn't believe he'll need it all too much.

"Once you start doing it," he said of election coverage in an Axios interview published Nov. 3, "the adrenaline of new results, new material, new information constantly coming in, it carries you."

Netflix Officially Revives Sylvester Stallone’s Beloved Action Thriller Before the Remake

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Feel free to connect with him or check out his work. He's everywhere — Upwork, YouTube, Spotify, SoundCloud, Collider, LinkedIn, Instagram.

2026 has been a very good year to be a Sylvester Stallone fan. I Play Rocky, the story of how Stallone wrote the Rocky screenplay and bet everything on himself to play the lead against impossible odds, recently premiered at TIFF and is already being hailed as one of the best movies in an already stacked year. Then Stallone fans got another treat as well, when he revealed that he has completed the script for Rocky 7. Apart from the Rocky universe, Stallone has also been enjoying success on TV with the recently released fourth season of Taylor Sheridan's Tulsa King. Now, another Stallone favorite just got an exciting update.

The film in question is a ‘90s cult classic survival thriller with Stallone as a mountain-rescue expert. A plane heist goes wrong, the aircraft blows up, and three suitcases holding $100 million are scattered across the mountains. The criminals then fake a distress call to lure Stallone’s character and his former partner into the wilderness, and force them to use their climbing skills to track down the missing money.

The film is none other than Cliffhanger, which, ironically, doesn’t end on one. It was directed by Renny Harlin, the same filmmaker behind other beloved classics like Deep Blue Sea and Die Hard 2, and it was a huge blockbuster hit when it came out in theaters. The film grossed a whopping $255 million worldwide against a $70 million budget. Now, it is about to gain a whole new life because it has finally landed on Netflix as of November 1.

This newfound attention is going to be great, because the franchise is being rebooted as a feature film slated for 2027. Stallone was initially attached to a direct sequel, but a major creative overhaul in 2024 led to his departure, and he is completely disconnected from this one. With Stallone gone, the reboot needs all the goodwill it can get, and a Netflix debut is a great way to remind longtime fans why they loved the original.

The upcoming reboot will be directed by Jaume Collet-Serra, who has already proven his directing chops with thrillers like Carry-On, House of Wax, and Orphan. Early plot details pitch the reboot as a father-daughter take on Die Hard. It’ll be a kidnapping story where veteran mountaineer Ray Cooper (Pierce Brosnan) is taken hostage on his weekend retreat, and his daughter Naomi (Lily James) has to face her fears and cross the brutal alpine peaks to save her family.

Cliffhanger is available to stream now on Netflix.

Netflix Officially Adapting Forgotten Bestselling Series After 20-Year Wait

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Growing up around performers, storytelling was always central to Emma's life. Add in a nerdy older brother who introduced her to Marvel at far too young an age, and her love for entertainment led her to pursue a Journalism degree at Indiana University, where she discovered her passion for entertainment reporting. She spent nearly one year in Los Angeles covering industry stories and fully immersing herself in the world of entertainment media.

Netflix is finally moving forward with a young adult drama from the creator of the television series One Day.

Based on Curtis Sittenfeld's bestselling 2005 novel, Prep follows Lee Fiora, a scholarship student attending a prestigious New England boarding school. The coming-of-age story explores Lee's struggles with friendships, romance, and class differences as she attempts to fit in among her wealthy classmates. The project comes more than two decades after the novel's publication and a previous attempt to adapt the story for television.

Deadline now reports that Netflix has officially given Prep a straight-to-series order, with One Day creator Nicole Taylor attached as writer, showrunner, and executive producer. Will Ferrell, Jessica Elbaum, and Alix Taylor will also executive produce through Gloria Sanchez Productions, alongside Sittenfeld. The half-hour drama is expected to open its writers' room in January 2027. Taylor, who has been hoping to adapt the novel for 20 years, shared her excitement about the announcement:

"Prep is my all time favourite novel and I have longed to adapt it for the entire twenty years since I first read it and found myself reflected uncannily back."

The project marks Taylor's latest Netflix adaptation following the success of One Day, which premiered in 2024. Based on David Nicholls' bestselling novel, the romantic drama starred Leo Woodall and Ambika Mod as Dexter Mayhew and Emma Morley, whose complicated relationship unfolds over two decades. One Day earned a 92% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes from 49 reviews, alongside an 85% audience score. The series received praise for its performances and emotional storytelling, giving Taylor another successful book adaptation ahead of Prep.

However, this isn't the first time Prep has been considered for a television adaptation. In 2016, HBO began developing a comedy series based on Sittenfeld's novel, with 30 Rock writer Colleen McGuinness and Game of Thrones executive producer Carolyn Strauss attached. The project ultimately never moved forward.

DC Studios Officially Casts Mason Thames As Robin — Will He Play Him In Live-Action?

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

We might not know when the DC Universe will unveil its version of Batman, but we just got news that the franchise has cast its Robin. Well, at least we know who'll be voicing one of the Robins in the upcoming "Dynamic Duo" movie. In an interview with Collider, DC Studios co-head Peter Safran confirmed that Mason Thames has been cast as the voice of Dick Grayson in the upcoming film.

Thames is best known for starring in "The Black Phone" and its sequel, as well as playing Hiccup Horrendous Haddock III in the live-action "How to Train Your Dragon" movie. But it seems he's been gunning for a DC role. "We always talk about wanting volunteers and not recruits to be part of the DCU, to be part of DC Studios," said Safran, "and Mason always wanted to be Dick Grayson. He just did."

According to the producer, Thames came in to read for Grayson and "auditioned the s*** out of it." "He's a wonderful young actor," he continued. "But boy, did he want this. He was just obsessed. When he came over to the DC offices, he came over with his dad at the time, and we've got tons of great archives and stuff in there, and wanted to take a picture of everything and with everything. He's a fan."

That certainly bodes well for a committed performance from the 19-year-old, who most recently appeared alongside Dave Franco and O'Shea Jackson Jr. in the most startling comedy of 2026, "Idiots." The question is, will Thames actually play Dick Grayson in live-action? According to past comments from Safran's DC Studios co-head James Gunn, that's exactly what will happen. But there's no confirmation as of yet.

"Dynamic Duo" is an animated film that's being developed by DC Studios in collaboration with Swaybox Studios. The latter's unique animation style combines puppetry and CGI to create a stop-motion-like effect that will be used to tell Jason Todd and Dick Grayson's origin stories. Mason Thames' casting as the latter had previously been rumored, with scooper Jeff Sneider reporting it in September 2026. But now it's official, meaning Thames will join Logan Kim, who's playing Jason Todd, in "Dynamic Duo" — much to the dismay of "Titans" star Curran Walters, who reacted to the news of the Jason Todd recasting with disappointment.

The movie is set for a September 22, 2028 release, with Arthur Mintz, who co-founded Swaybox with Theresa Andersson in 2013, directing. For now, further details about the film are scarce, but Lee Pace and Steve Buscemi have reportedly been cast as members of the Red Hood Gang.

As it stands, we at least know who will be voicing two of the most significant versions of Robin in the DC Universe. The question is, will Thames and Kim portray their characters in live-action? They certainly should, given James Gunn's prior comments.

When he and Peter Safran became co-heads of DC Studios in 2022, they promised this latest on-screen superhero franchise would not simply be more of the same. Gunn touted a shared universe without a house style and confirmed that DC Universe actors would portray their character across film, TV, animation, and even video games, streamlining the franchise in a way that sounded as though it could be bad news for animation voice actors. But it remains to be seen whether he will stick to that claim moving forward, especially since much has changed since 2022.

James Gunn has repeated his claim about actors portraying their DC Universe characters across mediums on several occasions. In 2023, he told DC.com "Television, movies and games all intertwine within the same universe. We're going to cast actors that are going to be able to play the characters in this, as well as in other things, some of which we've already cast." Thus far, Frank Grillo has played Rick Flag Sr. on the big screen in "Superman," on TV in "Peacemaker," and voiced the character for animation in "Creature Commandos." As such, it would make sense for Logan Kim and Mason Thames to follow a similar path.

But as we've seen in recent years, nothing is set in stone. Leaving aside the Warner Bros. Discovery/Paramount merger becoming official in October 2026, James Gunn and Peter Safran announced a slate of movies back in 2022, some of which have now been scrapped. Gunn confirmed that DC had cancelled the planned "The Authority" movie, for instance, and he has spoken openly about how plans for the DC Universe otherwise shifted following Warner Bros.' initial announcement. All of which raises the question of whether he's sticking to his plan for actor continuity across mediums.

It would certainly make a lot of sense given his previous explanations. In 2023, Gunn spoke to The Hollywood Reporter about how Aaron Pierre would be playing Green Lantern John Stewart in both the HBO series "Lanterns" and on the big screen in the upcoming "Man of Tomorrow." "We don't want the series to feel in any way like step-children or lesser-than," he told the outlet. "[The show is] just another way to tell a story." Time will tell if the same holds for Kim and Thames.

Sylvester Stallone’s Role In I Play Rocky Casting Confirmed By Director

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

I Play Rocky director Peter Farrelly reveals the role Sylvester Stallone played in the movie's casting.

The biopic about the making of Rocky features Anthony Ippolito playing both Stallone and the titular character. In terms of his involvement with the movie, Stallone previously said he was "blindsided" and "shocked to read [about] it. I have zero to do with it. Since I lived it, I thought I might be able to participate and give them some insight." Farrelly later shared that a script was sent to the original Rocky star and writer, who gave his blessing to proceed with the project.

In an interview with The Ankler, Farrelly has now explained that "when I met with Sylvester Stallone to get his blessing, he had one request. He goes, 'If you could, I'd like you to hire a relative unknown because that's what I was.' And I think that was part of the charm of the character is that nobody really knew him before."

At the time, Farrelly and his creative team had started discussing who should be cast, but the official process had not begun yet. Then "Anthony [Ippolito] heard about it, tracked down the script, put himself on tape in his grandmother's basement in Long Island, did three scenes, including 'the thing' scene at the end when he's at the door, and I sent it to Rick Montgomery, my casting agent. He sent it to me. I was like, that's it. It's over."

Ippolito starred in Adam Sandler's critically panned 2015 movie Pixels and played Al Pacino in Paramount+'s 2022 miniseries The Offer, but he was a relative unknown before being cast as Rocky. As for how Farrelly knew he was the right choice, he reflects on how "people say, 'How'd you know? What gave you the courage to take a chance on him?' I was like, 'I wasn't taking a chance. It was fully formed.'"

Farrelly continues, "This kid came in and he's a genius. He knew the difference between Stallone and Rocky, and there's a subtle difference . . . Anthony Ippolito didn't do a caricature of either. When you're looking at him as Stallone, it's like he is Stallone."

While Stallone was not directly involved in the casting process for I Play Rocky, his advice was taken into account and honored by casting Ippolito, with this being the 27-year-old actor's most high-profile role yet. Per Farrelly's explanation, the selection was about more than Stallone's advice, though, as it had more to do with Ippolito being able to seamlessly play both the real-life artist and the iconic fictional character he brought to life.

Following its premiere at the 2026 Toronto International Film Festival, the movie has received an 87% Tomatometer score based on 62 reviews. In ScreenRant's I Play Rocky review, George Bate gave it a rating of nine out of 10 stars. In terms of Ippolito's performance, he wrote that it "produces the kind of awards-worthy performance that actors make careers out of; there is more heart and humor in a single scene than most movies possess in their total runtime; and, perhaps most surprising of all, Farrelly creates a film that somehow matches the inspiration fostered by watching Rocky."

Choose the movie with the higher Rotten Tomatoes score.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

The positive reviews and awards buzz are promising for the film ahead of its limited theatrical debut on November 6, followed by a wide release on November 20. It appears to be a crowd-pleaser that will appeal to fans of the Rocky movies and of Stallone in general, especially given the praise for how Ippolito excels in these dual roles.

Ippolito's co-stars include AnnaSophia Robb as Sasha Czack, Matt Dillon as Frank Stallone Sr., P.J. Byrne as Irwin Winkler, Toby Kebbell as Robert Chartoff, Stephan James as Carl Weathers, Jay Duplass as John G. Avildsen, Robert Morgan as Burgess Meredith, Kiki Seto as Talia Shire, Scot Teller as Burt Young, and Tracy Letts as Sandy Maddox. I Play Rocky was written by Peter Gamble and directed by Farrelly.

Fox’s 107-Episode Version Of The Pitt Leaves Netflix In 3 Weeks

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In the current streaming landscape, it's hard to think of a bigger medical drama than The Pitt, but perhaps its biggest competition is a six-season Fox series that's about to leave Netflix. Premiering on HBO Max in 2025, ER's Noah Wyle re-dons his scrubs to lead the cast of The Pitt as Dr. Michael "Robby" Robinavitch, a senior attending physician at Pittsburgh Trauma Medical Center. The Pitt has aired two seasons to date, each set in real time over a 15-hour emergency room shift.

Not only has the medical drama earned back-to-back Primetime Emmy wins for best drama series and a 96% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes, but The Pitt has been universally praised by real-life medical professionals for its unmatched clinical accuracy and its addressing of systemic healthcare issues, all while being one of the most entertaining shows on TV and never feeling preachy. Whereas most popular medical dramas capture audiences with addictive romantic storylines (Grey's Anatomy) or cerebral mysteries (House), The Pitt's hook is, simply, authenticity.

In that regard, there's no show like it, but The Resident comes the closest. Set at the fictional Chastain Park Memorial Hospital in Atlanta, Georgia, The Resident's cast is led by Matt Czuchry as Dr. Conrad Hawkins, the titular resident who uses unconventional, often rule-breaking methods to get the job done and save lives while battling the ultimate antagonist—the profit-driven hospital administration. And soon, the show will be leaving its streaming home.

The Resident's six-season network run is an impressive feat, but the medical drama really became a pop culture mainstay when it arrived on Netflix US in March 2024, just under a year after Fox canceled it. The series debuted at No. 2 of Netflix's most-watched series before climbing to the top spot, with 1.18 billion minutes of total viewing time in just under two months (via THR).

This post-cancellation "Netflix bump" is almost as big as the record-breaking success that Suits enjoyed in 2023, with many new fans clamoring for the streamer to revive The Resident for a seventh season. While this never came to fruition, Netflix became synonymous with the popular medical drama, similar to shows like Gilmore Girls or Schitt's Creek that also found renewed success on the platform.

After its season 6 cancellation, The Resident star Manish Dayal discusses the possibility of a season 7 revival after the show's success on Netflix.

This is what makes it so heartbreaking that The Resident is leaving Netflix on November 20, 2026. While the show isn't disappearing from streaming entirely, as it will find a new home on Hulu and Disney+, there's no denying that this is the end of an era for Dr. Conrad Hawkins and co.

Even though The Resident will still be around, it's still leaving the world's biggest streaming platform, making it less of a rival for The Pitt. With Netflix having canceled its own attempt at a The Pitt replacement, Pulse, after only one season, the only real Netflix competition the Emmy-winning HBO show has is Grey's Anatomy.

The Shondaland series has been on the platform for a whopping 17 years, having arrived on Netflix well before it became the streaming juggernaut it is now. Seasons 1 through 22 are available to stream, while the newest season, Grey's Anatomy season 23, is currently airing on Thursdays at 10 PM ET on ABC, with next-day streaming on Hulu. Netflix is expected to get season 23 in June 2027, approximately one month after the season finale.

Choose the movie with the higher Rotten Tomatoes score.

Fortunately, unlike The Resident, it doesn't look like Grey's Anatomy will be leaving Netflix anytime soon. So, while The Pitt may reign supreme, it still has some medical drama competition.

The 10 Greatest Horror Thriller Opening Scenes, Ranked

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Horror thrillers are a special kind of horror that needs to grip you in the plot right from the very beginning. It is essential to set up the story, build the suspense, and attract the audience's attention right from the opening scene in all genres of cinema. Horror particularly needs this, resulting in some of our favorite opening moments of all time.

The opening scene, when done right, can fill the viewers in on what they can expect and enjoy the absolute wonder they're about to experience for the rest of the story. Sometimes, the opening surpasses the rest of the film even. Horror has no short list of the most compelling opening scenes, but the best ones truly come from when horror meets thriller. No better genres mix suspense, atmosphere, and bone-chilling dread than these two when combined. They've resulted in some of the best openings horror has to offer. From one nail-biting sequence to the next, here are the ten best first scenes in horror-thriller history.

Ari Aster established himself as the king of thematic horror thanks to his 2019 cult classic Midsommar. This eerie slow-burner is the modern equivalent of Robin Hardy's The Wicker Man, combining slow-moving tension and shocking rug pullers. But unlike its iconic inspiration, Midsommar hits the ground running with one hell of a brutal opening tragedy.

The film starts with the protagonist, Dani (Florence Pugh), beginning her spiraling descent into grief and madness when her mentally struggling sister kills her parents and herself via carbon monoxide poisoning. It's a gun-punching opener highlighted by a slow, quiet first few moments followed by an intense transition to a graphic mass murder, accompanied by haunting music. This scene perfectly sets up the unexpected brutality of Aster's unique film.

The pinnacle of horror in the late 2010s, Jordan Peele's remarkable filmmaking debut, Get Out, is the modern masterpiece that so many films since have continued to reach for greatness. A thriller that constantly keeps you on the edge of your seat, its greatest strengths come from its ambiguity and unconventional storytelling. Hardly anywhere does this shine more than in its eerie and rather strange opener.

Instead of a gruesome kill or frightening introduction of an antagonist, it opens with an ordinary Black man (Lakeith Stanfield) walking down a dark street when he's kidnapped by a mysterious man in a knight's helmet. It doesn't establish the plot, and we don't even know who this young man is; we just witness a bizarre kidnapping that we don't get an answer for until much later in the story. Though this might seem unusual, it perfectly grips you into a one-of-a-kind psychological story that will surely leave you speechless by the end.

Horror saw an unbelievable change at the turn of the 21st century with James Wong's Final Destination. Easily one of the most paranoia-inducing horror films in history, this incredible nail-biter changed the game with its unique premise and unrelenting suspense, making viewers scared that anything terrible could happen at any moment. And it all starts with a highly tense opening scene, which has become cemented in horror history.

When high schooler Alex Browning (Devon Sawa) experiences a premonition of a horrific disaster before his class trip's plane takes off, he freaks out and escapes the departure along with a lucky few shortly before the plane actually explodes. This opener has become synonymous with tension building. It's perfectly set up through the use of subtle ominous foreshadowing and eerie leitmotifs, and the terrifying punch comes when Alex's vision comes true. It's a well-crafted, successful opener that jump-started this iconic horror franchise with a legendary boost.

Before reinventing the DC Universe, Zack Snyder dipped his hands in horror, and boy did he give it a considerable contribution when revitalizing the popularity of one of the greatest zombie movies of all time, Dawn of the Dead. His 2004 remake of the late George Romero's masterpiece is certainly more fast-paced, action-packed and intense than its 1978 original. The wild, chaotic madness of its thrilling opener certainly affirms it.

In perhaps the greatest opening scene in zombie history, our lead heroine, Ana (Sarah Polley), awakens to a world rapidly descending into chaos as the dead are quickly rising. This opener doesn't hold back, dropping you right into the confusing mayhem and keeping you feeling completely on edge. While arguably the rest of the film doesn't match the intensity of this iconic introductory sequence, it's still a perfect starter that hardly any other zombie film can top.

John Krasinski's A Quiet Place is the thrilling sci-fi chiller that continues to define horror in the 21st century. With a unique, frightening concept, endless suspense, and a constant sense of impending doom, this 2018 masterpiece knows how to scare us out of our wits, and it first achieved this with a suddenly tragic opener that continues to give us nightmares.

In a world devastated by an invading hostile alien species known as "The Death Angels," the Abbott family, led by their struggling patriarch Lee (Krasinski), are forced to adapt to this new way of living while trying not to make too much noise, or else attract the attention of these predators. Unfortunately, one crucial mistake leads their poor youngest, Beau (Cade Woodward), to suffer a gruesome fate right in front of them. This opening kill hits viewers like a truck, instantly establishing the severity of what happens when these characters make a sound. It's an unflinching and sudden gut-puncher that perfectly sets this story off on a high note.

Hailed as one of the scariest monster thrillers of all time, 2014's It Follows takes the cake for mounting dread and suspense. It is a masterclass in tension, telling a fresh, original, and horrifying story of an unstoppable entity slowly but surely walking towards its victim. Even its very first scene quickly establishes just how inescapable and bone-chillingly unsettling this idea is.

It starts with a young woman, Annie (Bailey Spry), chaotically rushing out of her house and across her neighborhood alone while looking around like something is walking straight towards her. The audience doesn't see through her perspective, nor is there an indication that something actually is following her. By the end of this scene, it cuts to a shocking image of Annie's mangled corpse on a beach. This startling scene quickly sets up the story but doesn't give anything away. It's a brilliant approach that instantly hooks the viewer into figuring out what did this to that poor girl.

The film that changed slashers forever, John Carpenter's indie horror masterpiece Halloween reshaped this legendary subgenre into the dominant force it is today. Featuring iconic moments and characters that have become synonymous with horror, Halloween gave audiences an unforgettably eerie thriller that kept them on the edge of their seats right off the bat.

In perhaps one of the most famous twists in all of cinema, the film starts from the perspective of an unknown killer as they slowly stalk and murder poor teenager Judith Myers (Sandy Johnson) in her home, before stepping outside to be unmasked by his parents as six-year-old Michael Myers. From Carpenter's eerie score to the unsettling tone and uninterrupted suspense, this perfect opener set up what made Halloween such a cherished icon of horror. It's a masterclass in using mood, cinematography, and music to create the best kind of terror.

Your instincts, your strengths, and your particular way of thinking under pressure point to one villain you actually have a fighting chance against. Everyone else — good luck.

Jason is relentless, but he is also predictable — and that is the gap you would exploit.

Michael watches before he moves. He is patient, methodical, and almost impossible to detect — until it's too late for anyone who isn't paying close enough attention.

Freddy wins by getting inside your head — using your own fears, your own memories, your own subconscious as weapons against you. That strategy requires a target who can be destabilised.

Pennywise is ancient, shapeshifting, and feeds on terror — but it has one critical vulnerability: it cannot function against someone who genuinely stops being afraid of it.

Chucky's greatest advantage is that nobody takes him seriously until it's already too late. He exploits the gap between how something looks and what it actually is.

John Carpenter continued to produce memorable horror openers in the 20th century as his next big masterpiece, The Thing, kicked things off with a compelling mystery. Widely considered the greatest horror movie of all time by many, The Thing has been hailed for creating endless terror and suspense from beginning to end.

It starts with utter chaos as the beautiful Antarctic tundra silence is interrupted when two Norwegians in a helicopter shoot at a defenseless snow dog running towards an American base. As the Norwegians continue to shoot at the dog while at the base, the confused Americans are forced to kill them in self-defense, but it seems clear the Norwegians were targeting that strange-acting dog for a dire reason. This opening scene is much like the rest of the film: ominous, ambiguous, and truly unsettling. It keeps audiences hooked and wanting to watch more of what's happening.

All hail one of the most influential horror classics, Scream, released in 1996. This Wes Craven-helmed groundbreaker reinvented the slasher subgenre at a point when it needed it the most. Terrifying, darkly hilarious, and sharply witty thanks to a superb Kevin Williamson script, it first drew audiences in with a now-legendary opening kill that has been cemented in pop culture.

It all begins with a phone call. Woodsboro high school student Casey Becker (Drew Barrymore) answers a call one night from a mysterious voice who quickly goes from friendly to sinister, forcing her to play his sadistic game of horror movie trivia before ultimately killing her and her tied-up boyfriend Steve (Kevin Patrick Walls) while in a Ghostface costume. Scream's opening has been praised for its intensity and perfect pacing, dropping viewers into an unforgettable story that they certainly won't want to miss. It's tense, suspenseful, and utterly thrilling, greatly starting this bona fide masterpiece on the strong note that it deserved.

Finally, the top spot goes to arguably the most iconic and frightening opener in horror history. Released in 1975 and directed by the great Steven Spielberg, Jaws is the monumental horror blockbuster that boosted the genre to new heights of box office dominance. It's undoubtedly a thrilling roller-coaster from start to finish, beginning with a legendary first kill that has become immortalized in cinema history.

While out for a peaceful night swim, poor Chrissie Watkins (Susan Backlinie) swims a little too far out and unknowingly attracts a mysterious predator lurking in the depths. Suddenly, the unseen creature strikes and drags her screaming below the waves. Jaws' opening scene cemented a place in pop culture for being a masterclass in build-up and suspense. From John Williams' iconic score to the unique POV shots of the shark as it rises up out of the depths, this one ultimately takes the crown as the best horror thriller opening of all time.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Dior and <i><em>Harper’s Bazaar</em></i> Host a Screening of the New Richard Avedon Documentary in Los Angeles

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

With Emma Watson, Gia Coppola, and more in attendance

From a Paris Fashion Week toast to a New York City launch party, Dior and Harper’s Bazaar have been honoring the legacy of fashion photographer Richard Avedon these past few weeks. This series of intimate events has been tied to the launch of a new coffee-table book titled Richard Avedon: The Harper’s Bazaar Years, as well as a documentary film, Avedon. Now, the festivities have traveled to Los Angeles.

Avedon, who passed away in 2004 at the age of 81, was one of the most influential fashion photographers of the 20th century. In 1944, at just 21 years old, he started his career at Harper’s Bazaar under the guidance of then-editor-in-chief Carmel Snow, and he spent the next two-plus decades defining the creative vision of the publication.

Richard Avedon: The Harper’s Bazaar Years (available now) explores this artistic period by compiling rarely seen photographs, a never-before-published account from Avedon himself, a foreword by editor-in-chief Samira Nasr, and an essay by Bazaar contributing editor Derek C. Blasberg. The new documentary, meanwhile, offers a look through the Avedon archives from a different lens. It was directed by Academy Award–winning filmmaker Ron Howard (Apollo 13, A Beautiful Mind, The Da Vinci Code).

Focusing on the film, Dior and Blasberg hosted a private screening of Avedon at the William Morris Endeavor headquarters in L.A. Guests such as Emma Watson, Gia Coppola, Lauren Hutton, Devon Lee Carlson, and more gathered to watch the film before heading to the House of Dior in Beverly Hills for a cocktail reception.

Ahead, we offer an intimate look inside the event.

Man United conjured a miraculous comeback against Roma that was thrilling, visceral and raw... finally Michael Carrick has something to cling to, writes IAN LADYMAN

📅 2026-11-03  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Published: 17:15 EDT, 3 November 2026 | Updated: 17:24 EDT, 3 November 2026

Michael Carrick, for once, has something other than desperate calamity to talk about for the next few days. The build-up to Sunday’s game here against Aston Villa will instead be dominated by reflections on the kind of unlikely and fabulous drama we used to witness at Old Trafford all the time.

A comeback so thrilling and raw and visceral that it will have kept his players up all night, Carrick at last has something to cling to as he tries to drag Manchester United’s season back from the jaws of hell.

How do we describe this game? Maybe just with the facts.

Carrick’s side were so bad in the first half it was almost impossible to understand. However awful they have been this season, this was worse. Two down to early goals from Matias Soule and Donyell Malen, United presented both to Roma on a plate.

The game looked over. Carrick’s reign felt over. In the crowd they waved green and gold balloons in protest at the owners the Glazers.

Matheus Cunha's late double rescued United from the jaws of defeat on a night of madness at Old Trafford

Cunha scored in the 83rd and 86th minute to turn the game on its head

There was still time for Cunha to be sent off in the dying embers of the game - but that failed to take the shine off a memorable night

But somehow, almost impossibly, this game changed. Whether it will be enough to prompt a genuine change in the course of United’s season, only time will tell. The chances are it won’t. There is too much wrong. But let’s see.

United were lucky, too. They were lucky to be given an early second-half penalty and lucky that Bruno Fernandes was given the chance to take it again after he missed the first one. They were then lucky in the 83rd minute when Matheus Cunha was handed an equaliser by a defensive howler.

But luck is no good if you don’t ride it. And the winning goal Cunha delivered into the net at the near post with four minutes left was born of desire, desperation and perhaps some anger after the pain of the last few weeks. Within a second, the Brazilian’s shirt was off and he was in the crowd.

It was hard to blame Cunha, even if he was to pay a price. Booked for that, he was cautioned again for a foul that soon followed and was forced to leave the scene early. At least it was to an ovation. We have not heard one of those here for a while.

Earlier, Old Trafford had been flat for a while. United, for once, resisted the urge to blow their own toes off early on as they had done in conceding poor early goals at Atletico Madrid and Como in previous European away games.

But the problem with this team is that it only takes one spark to light a fire big enough to burn the house down. So it was once again here.

Matias Soule had given Roma the lead after only 11 minutes

The game was briefly halted in the first-half when fans threw objects including tennis balls onto the pitch

United boss Michael Carrick suffered a further blow when Carlos Baleba limped off

There was nothing in this game for the first ten minutes and then it all went wrong once again. From safety to dire straits and the verge of mutiny in the space of seven minutes.

The first Roma goal was extraordinary. When United midfielder Kobbie Mainoo lost the ball in his own half, his team found themselves a defender short. Diogo Dalot, the left back, was so far upfield he may as well have been holding a balloon on the Stretford End.

Quite why this was the case nobody knew but when Rodrigo Mora switched the ball across into the space where Dalot should have been, Soule had time to cut inside and shoot low in to Senne Lammens’ bottom left hand corner.

It was one of those goals that looked stranger the more you studied it. Six minutes later, United presented Roma with another one.

Again confusion reigned at the back and once again Dalot was out of position as the ball reached the goalscorer on the edge of the penalty area.

This time only a sliding block from Leny Yoro denied Soule his second goal. Sadly for the United defender, the ball had struck his raised arm and former Aston Villa forward Malen slid in the penalty.

Around Old Trafford, mutiny hung in the air. Aswell as their balloons, some fans had brought tennis balls with them too. As they were hurled on to the field, the only surprise was that one of the United players didn’t welly one into his own net.

As half-time neared, Malen brought a low save from Lammens before Soule curled one from 18 yards that must have missed the left hand post by no more than six inches.

That was the difference between United being out of the game and still in with the chance of the kind of comeback an early second half goal would bring.

Sure enough, Old Trafford soon got the lift it wanted. Cunha seemed fortunate to be awarded a penalty just before the hour – he seemed to trip himself up – while Fernandes’ first spot-kick was saved by former Sheffield Wednesday keeper – and Roma’s third choice – Devis Vasquez. VAR spotted encroachment by three Roma defenders, though, and Fernandes smashed the retake high into the net.

Roma now faced a different home team and indeed a different environment. Immediately, United almost levelled. Cunha played Bryan Mbeumo clear and his lifted shot seemed destined to find the net only for the ball to strike both posts and somehow stay out.

Without doubt United were lucky to back in it. Two big moments had gone their way. Their response was nevertheless appropriate. Finally we saw some energy and belief and fundamental desire.

Bruno Fernandes' first penalty was saved - but he made amends with his second after the referee ordered a retake for encroachment

Victory eases the pressure on United boss Michael Carrick

Camped now in the Roma half, United attempted to lay siege. Harry Maguire’s header from a corner bounced off an opponent. From the next one, the ball ran free near the goal line and a defender hacked clear. United were soon appealing for a penalty at the far post but instead Roma got a free-kick. It was now that kind of game.

Vasquez saved low from Cunha with 18 minutes left but United had to wait another six minutes for the ending that matched anything we have seen here for quite some time.

A cross from Fernandes should have been cleared by Roma defender Leonardo Balerdi but his right foot connected only with air and Cunha slid the ball in from six yards.

Roma were by now a shell of the team that they had been in the first half. And when Cunha unleashed his shot from an angle three minutes later they were dead.
